Dan White, City Attorney.  The pledge of allegiance was recited.
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA]  are considered to be
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its
normal sequence on the agenda.) Upon request by Ms. Ash, and pursuant to support received by Mr.
Collins and Dr. Rinne, agenda item #22(c) was removed from the Consent Agenda. Mr. Collins
moved to approve remaining Consent Agenda items, seconded by Mr. Brown.  M otion carried.

“AN ORDINANCE AMENDING ORDINANCE NO. 3860 DEFINING THE TERMS AND
CONDITIONS OF EMPLOYMENT FOR NON-UNIFORMED EMPLOYEES OF THE CITY OF
CHEYENNE.”  E ric H ardgrave, C heyenne P ublic E mployees A ssociation ( CPEA) P resident,
discussed  job loyalty by City employees, cost savings ideas that had been submitted to the Mayor
for consideration, opposition to reduction in workforce, and concern with proposed employee loss
of benefits, particularly longevity pay.  The following individuals expressed concerns regarding the
proposed ordinance amendments:  Nathan Maine, Parks Division; Mark Pollard, Golf Division; John
Palmer, San itation De pt.; Kelly K enworthy, 1% Co nstruction E ngineering; Dav e Spo rts, 1%
Construction Engineering; Steve Pennock, Sanitation; Donn Edmunds, citizen; Ron Conard, Golf
Division, and Dick Mason, Building Dept.  Topics discussed included: Dividing the p roposed
ordinance amendments to allow for separate discussion; researching and reviewing various areas
for cost savings, including energy savings options, solar/wind energy, energy grants, land sales, fuel
usage, and cell phone usage and plans; voluntary time off by higher paid employees; hiring freeze,
furlough days; opposition to removal of longevity pay program; review areas involving revenue;
employee survey to be generated by the 2009 Leadership class with the support of CPEA to acquire
input and suggestions from employees on cost savings or revenue generation; support for furlough
days before implementing a reduction in workforce or other options involving wages; suggestion
of bringing BOPU “into the fold”; concern with recent softball field agreement with VFW relative
to c osts; r estoration ti me frame for a ny b enefits w hich may b e lost; e mployees’ c oncerns w ith
communication is sues r egarding t heir in put, s uggestions a nd t he a nticipated b udget s hortfall;
allowing t ime f or a reas i nvolving c ost r eductions/efficiencies by  d epartments t o d etermine
effectiveness; review of police vehicle policy;  reducing construction and materials costs; statement
that furloughs and reductions in workforce doesn’t reduce employee workloads, and suggestion of
possible reduction in pay option predicated o n reassurance monies would be restored when the
financial situation improves.  R eporting for Finance Committee, Mr. Brown stated there was no
recommendation on the item from the Committee. Mr. Collins moved to approve on second reading,
seconded by Mr. Brown.  Mr. Collins moved to amend Section 4 and Exhibit B to restore longevity
pay pro visions, s econded b y M s. Bro yles.  Upo n i nquiry, City T reasurer Barb Do rr rev iewed
anticipated budget shortfall amounts for the current fiscal year and potential shortfall for next fiscal
year, stating the amount will depend on upcoming state legislative decisions.  She also provided
information on the City’s use of 5th penny sales tax dollars.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes”
– all members of the governing body.  Discussion followed on concerns with the next fiscal year
budget a nd s tate f unding a ssistance to m unicipalities; employee c ost s avings i deas ( submit to
Mayor); e nergy e fficiency e fforts a nd lack o f p ast d ue d iligence in r esearching and c onserving
savings; past employee pay increases; City reserves; cell phone plan review to ascertain savings;
energy savings and wind generation options, staff overtime costs, potential early retirement offering,
and r eview o f a ll bu dget a reas i n c onjunction w ith e mployee i deas f or cost savings.  D uring
discussion City Attorney Dan White confirmed there presently is no single cell phone contract plan
and Mayor Kaysen advised a city-wide review of cell phone usage was conducted in June of 2009
and that a point of control is now being utilized and a competitive provider for services process is
underway.  Upon inquiry regarding a possible early retirement offering, Ms. Dorr advised a review
of  employees eligible for retirement under the Rule of 85, pursuant to the state retirement program,
reflects that if all of the qualified individuals were to retire, $2,000,000 would be saved during the
year. S he s tated a  p ossible e arly r etirement p lan i s c urrently b eing r eviewed.  U pon i nquiry
regarding re venue e nhancement a reas t o a ssist with the an ticipated b udget s hortfall, M s. D orr
advised all departments reviewed areas during last spring’s budget preparation and many fee areas
had been increased.  She advised the City is limited in its ability to generate revenue with regard to
the amount o f property t ax assessed and o ther areas restricted by law.  U pon inquiry regarding
possible furlough days and the closing of City offices, Mayor Kaysen advised the issue involving
furlough d ays a ffiliated w ith c losure o f City offices t o t he p ublic h ad b een lo oked a t c losely
regarding potential energy/utility savings, but there may not be a lot of savings to be realized due
to the demand on utilities to “power back up”.  He explained furlough time on a flexible schedule
basis p er d epartment ( with offices r emaining o pen) w ould save money w hile still c ontinuing to
provide services to the community.  Mayor Kaysen indicated work would continue on a possible
early retirement program and noted there are federal laws that must be reviewed and complied with
relative to timing issues and notification to eligible employees.  With regard to revenue generation,
Mayor Kaysen clarified leases a re a lso being r eviewed involving u se o f City a thletic fields a nd
programs and provided information on a “pay for play” program that is being considered for a more
balanced approach for users as well as for City maintenance and operation costs.  Upon inquiry
regarding the status of debts/fees owed to the City, Ms. Dorr indicated there is money in arrears
owed by customers.  Discussion followed on employee cost savings ideas, with input provided by
Mr. Hardgrave (CPEA) upon request; developing an early retirement option that would determine
the greatest incentive to employees to retire at the lowest cost to the City; percentage of general fund
monies involving employee costs and benefits; monetary furlough effect on employees and potential
financial “crippling”of the workforce; statement that reduction in workforce may be necessary, and
energy efficiency savings. Upon inquiry, Vicki Nemecek, Public Works Dept., provided information
on the current energy efficiency program, indicating an energy audit had been conducted and the
program is moving forward with grant applications.  She explained award of grant funding has very
recently been received in the amount of $547,800.00, which will be used for l ighting r etrofits,
sensors, an energy education element, and a high efficiency boiler, which should result in proposed
savings of at least $168,000 a y ear.  Sh e indicated two other grants have been applied for ,and
advised i t w ill ta ke s ome y ears f or th e p ayback on  s ome s avings to b e realized.  D iscussion
continued on state legislative funding to municipalities; information on state funding provided by
a recent Wyoming Association of Municipalities publication, and whether the state can provide any
“wiggle ro om” relative t o t he t iming o f reduced f unds t o be received within t he state’s biennial
budget. U pon inquiry, Ms. Dorr advised she w as not a ware of a ny scenario in w hich th e state
delayed funding reductions to the second year of a biennial budget instead of implementing within
the f irst budget year.  Main motion as amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all members o f t he
governing body.
Mr. Pierson moved to change the order of agenda business to bring forward Item #25(a) as
the next item of business, seconded by Ms. Broyles. Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members
of the governing body.  Mayor Kaysen stated the item, entitled Appointment of Brian N. Kozak as
Chief of Police for the City of Cheyenne,  wo uld be brought forward for action.  M ayor Kaysen
introduced M r. K ozak, wh o i ntroduced f amily an d f riends in attendance, pr ovided a b rief
background of  h is l aw e nforcement c areer a nd e xperience, a nd c ommented on  h is p riority of
working with and gaining t he t rust o f t he community, and working with department staff.  M r.
Spiker moved to approve the appointment, seconded by Mr. Valdez.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes”
– a ll m embers of  t he governing b ody w ith th e e xception of  M ayor K aysen w ho d id n ot v ote
(Mayoral appointment). Mayor Kaysen advised Mr. Kozak’s first official day of work would be
March 1 , when he will be sworn in at a ceremony to be held at 8:30 a.m. t hat day in Councilst
Chambers.
Following a ten minute recess, Mayor Kaysen reconvened the meeting at 8:10 p.m.

Stop Upgrades Project (Federal Transit Authority & American Reinvestment and Recovery Act).
Mr. Brown moved to a pprove, seconded by Mr. Valdez.  I t was advised the Mayor's Council for
People with Disabilities had expressed concerns regarding the bus stop locations in proximity to
certain stores.  Upon inquiry, Joe Dougherty, Transit Director, provided information on transit stops
located along certain store areas off Dell Range Blvd.  an d alternatives reviewed to make stops
easier and safer for both buses and passengers to access.  He advised concerns had been raised
regarding moving t he stops o ut t o Dell Range so lo cations were chosen with easier access and
accessibility with minimal walking distance for passengers. Upon inquiry, Laura Page, Engineering
Office, advised minimum ADA standards are being met and that  c omprises were made in some
areas i n lieu o f ha ving t o a cquire r ight-of-way p roperty.  C oncerns w ere e xpressed r egarding
accessibility f rom d esignated l ocations f or th ose individuals w ith d isabilities.  M r. D ougherty
provided i nformation o n b us stop s tandards rel ative t o us er des tination di stances, i ndicating,
although not a perfect situation, locations of the stops are a reasonable accommodation; reviewed
other st op locations involving Frontier Mall and the post o ffice near Co nverse Ave.; discussed
evaluation of bus shelter advertising, if funding for the shelters is available, and stated curb-to-curb
transit services are available as an option for individuals having special needs. Upon inquiry, Ms.
Page reviewed logistics involving the location of the bus shelter near the post office, and clarified
the contract was for an amount not to exceed $29,790.00 (ARRA funding).  Motion carried. Voting
"yes" -- all members of the governing body. (#5045)
Contract M odification N o. 4  t o C ontract No. 4 968 be tween t he C ity o f C heyenne a nd

members of the governing body with the exception of Mr. Pierson voting "no". (#4968)

requested by the City Attorney, be present at the executive session, and stated there would be no
action taken by the governing body as a result of the executive session, and that the regular City
Council meeting would officially conclude at the close of the executive session.  (Clerk’s Note:
Executive session convened at 9:15 p.m. following a brief recess.)
There be ing no  further bu siness t o c ome be fore the governing bo dy p rior t o executive
session, t he meeting was o fficially a djourned a t 1 0:37 p .m. following c losure o f t he executive
session.
 Submitted by,
 
 
 
 Carol Intlekofer, MMC
 City Clerk
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request.

 January 22, 2010

January 25, 2010
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning
at 6 :00 p .m. i n C ouncil C hambers.  Pr esent w ere: M AYOR - Richard L . K aysen; C OUNCIL
MEMBERS:  Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Ms. Case, Mr. Collins, Mr. Pierson, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Spiker
and Mr. Valdez.  Absent: Ms. Ash.  Also present: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk; Lois Huff, Deputy
City Treasurer, and Dan White, City Attorney.  The pledge of allegiance was recited.
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA]  are considered to be
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its
normal sequence on the agenda.) Upon request by Ms. Broyles, and pursuant to support received by
Mr Brown and Mr. Pierson, agenda item #18(c) was removed from the Consent Agenda. Mr. Collins
moved to approve remaining Consent Agenda items, seconded by Mr. Brown.  M otion carried.
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.
[CA] MINUTES FROM REGULAR MEETING OF THE GOVERNING BODY ON JANUARY
11, 2010.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried.
[CA] MINUTES FROM VOUCHERS MEETING OF THE GOVERNING BODY ON JANUARY
19, 2010.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried.
Mr. Brown moved to approve payment of vouchers as presented, seconded by Mr. Valdez.
Upon i nquiry, Deput y City T reasurer L ois Huf f an d Ci ty Cl erk Caro l In tlekofer r eviewed
information available for a voucher payable to Cheyenne Frontier Days ($100.00), annual payment
for placement of  wireless communication equipment.   Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members
of the governing body present.
“AN ORDINANCE AMENDING ORDINANCE NO. 3860 DEFINING THE TERMS AND
CONDITIONS OF EMPLOYMENT FOR NON-UNIFORMED EMPLOYEES OF THE CITY OF
CHEYENNE.”  E ric H ardgrave, C heyenne P ublic E mployees A ssociation ( CPEA) P resident,
expressed a ppreciation to Council f or l istening t o e mployees’ c oncerns r egarding be nefits a nd
employment issues, and for restoring the longevity pay plan.  Edward Melko expressed concerns
with Council members’ eligibility for City health care coverage, suggested Council members accept
reduced compensation due to budgetary concerns, and commented on assessing recreational fees for
youth programs relative to the recent lease agreement approved for VFW’s Pride Park softball fields.
It was noted that not all Council members participate in the health insurance plan. Claus Johnson,
Parks & Recreation employee, pr ovided co mments o n budget co ncerns, red uction o f employee
benefits, effect o n services pro vided if staffing levels are reduced, an d po tential furloughs.  He
suggested deferral of a scheduled $1,000,000 BOPU payment due to the current budget situation,
and discussed the Salary Task Force Committee’s recent proposal involving a compensation and
staffing level study, employee cost savings suggestions, and equitable compensation to governing
body members relative to employee compensation cuts.  John Palmer, Sanitation Dept. employee,
expressed appreciation for restoring the longevity pay plan; commented on state projections and
budget concerns for the next fiscal year; requested consideration of employee cost savings/efficiency
ideas; expressed that employees are dedicated to serving the City and community, and encouraged
careful review of expenditures.  Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. Brown moved to approve
on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Valdez.  Mr. Brown moved to amend Section 7, by
inserting the language “after consulting with City Council leadership,” following the words, “The
Mayor”.  The section would then read “The Mayor, after consulting with City Council leadership,
shall have discretionary authority to declare furlough days, if necessary, due to budget constraints,”
seconded by Mr. Pierson.  Comments followed in opposition to the proposed amendment relative
to Council oversight of the Mayor’s executive administrative authority, as well as comments in
support of the amendment which would provide for communication from the Mayor to Council on
any possible employee furlough days authorized by the Mayor.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting
“yes” – all members of the governing body present with the exception of Ms. Case, Dr Rinne and
Mr. Valdez voting “no”.  Main motion as amended carried.  V oting “yes” – all members of the
governing body present. (#3880)
“AN ORDINANCE AMENDING ORDINANCE NO. 3861 DEFINING THE TERMS AND
CONDITIONS OF EMPLOYMENT FOR SWORN OFFICERS OF THE CHEYENNE POLICE
DEPARTMENT.”  Mr. Brown moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by M r.
Valdez.  Mr. Brown  moved to amend Section 8, by inserting the language “after consulting with
City Council leadership,” following the words, “The Mayor”.  The section would then read “The
Mayor, after consulting with City Council leadership, shall have discretionary authority to declare
furlough days, if necessary, due to budget constraints,” seconded by Mr. Pierson.  Com ments
followed in opposition to Council overseeing the authority of the Mayor who serves as the City’s
executive b ranch o f go vernment, an d co ncern wi th t he ph rase “City Council l eadership” v s.
members o f t he e ntire C ity C ouncil.  B rief d iscussion followed o n t he p roposed a mendment
providing that notification be received from the Mayor prior to any furlough days being authorized.
Motion to amend carried.  V oting “yes” – all members o f t he governing body present with the
exception of Ms. Case, Dr. Rinne and Mr. Valdez voting “no”.  Main motion as amended carried.
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present. (#3881)

commented on the company’s support and involvement (mutual aid) with the county Fire District
and industrial wat er r ates r eceived from th e B oard of  P ublic U tilities, a nd a dvised th at b y n ot

supported the proposed County Final Plat, as did the County Planning Commission and the City
Planning Commission.  He noted the staff report indicated there were no comments from City Police
or Fire Depts. pursuant to the proposed plat and potential impact to City services, and encouraged
members of the governing body to review the minutes from the City Planning Commission meeting.
Paula Qualls, Board member, South Cheyenne Community Development Assoc., and member of
the County Planning Commission, spoke in support of the plat, and in opposition to annexation.
Larry Wolfe, Holland & Hart law firm, attorneys for Frontier Refining, spoke in support of the plat
and clarified the item before the governing body pertains to approval of the plat involving an 11 acre
parcel to be utilized for storage and parking.  He stated further conversations could be engaged in
between the City and Frontier regarding annexation issues and effects.  Mr. Collins moved to adopt,
seconded by Mr. Brown.  Mr. Collins moved to amend by including the substitute map, seconded
by Dr. Rinne.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.
Discussion followed on support for Frontier Refining and the proposed plat; Urban Planning staff’s

“A RESOLU TION DE TERMINING THA T CO NDITIONS F OR A NNEXATION
REQUIRED BY WYO. STAT.§15-1-402 EXIST F OR THE PR OPERTY DESCRIBED AS A
PORTION O F SEC TIONS 1 2 A ND 1 3, T. 13N., R . 6 7W., O F TH E 6  P. M ., L ARAMIETH

INCLUDING CHEYENNE ANIMAL SHELTER AND ASSOCIATED RIG HTS-OF-WAY).”
Brad Emmons, AVI, agent for the applicants, advised pursuant to a request for i nformation
concerning contiguity made at Public Services Committee, he had submitted an exhibit regarding

any area landowners’ intentions if opposed to the annexation.  Donn Edmunds questioned access

property owners requested annexation.  Discussion included potential future access to the area by
the developer and, upon inquiry, Gay Woodhouse, attorney representing individuals who live on
Southwest Dri ve, adv ised t hey are co ncerned about acc ess areas  an d are rel ying o n prev ious
information provided, during comments at prior meetings, involving issues that would be reviewed
during the development and platting process. She stated litigation is still ongoing over a previous
matter relative to annexation of Southwest Drive.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of
the governing body present with the exception of Ms. Case and Mr. Valdez voting “no”.  (#5182)
“A RESOLUTION AUTHORIZING THE MAYOR TO OFFER FINANCIAL INCENTIVES
TO INDUCE CERTAIN ELIGIBLE EMPLOYEES TO RETIRE FROM CITY SERVICE DURING
FISCAL YEAR 2009-2010” (sponsors - Mr. Collins, Mr. Pierson and Mr. Spiker) -- referred to
Finance Committee.  Mr. Brown advised the item was discussed under other items for the agenda at
Finance Committee and provisions of Title 2, City Code, had been met.  Mr. Brown moved to adopt,
seconded by  M r. C ollins.  E ric H ardgrave, Cheyenne P ublic E mployees As sociation ( CPEA)
President, e xpressed a ppreciation f or the i ncentive p ackage b eing p roposed to h elp r educe or
potentially e liminate t he need for furloughs, a nd r equested t hat furloughs be u tilized before a ny
employee lay-offs are considered.  Edward Melko spoke in opposition to use of the sanitation fund
(i.e. Solid waste management fund) as the funding source for the retirement incentives, expressing
his reasons for disagreement with the funding source, and questioned the amount of City reserves.
Questions f ollowed regardi ng appro priate us es o f t he s olid was te f und.  Up on i nquiry, Vi cki
Nemecek, Asst. Public Works Director, provided information on the resolution establishing the solid
waste fund, use of the funds, and current fund balance ($8.8 million).  She advised $2.9 million was
expended for t he B elvoir Ran ch, an d t hat t he B elvoir co uld po tentially b e t he n ew landfill s ite.
Public Works Director Jim Elias advised staff is working on an overall solid waste management plan
and is moving through the process to propose options for a new landfill site, which may or may not
be located on the Belvoir.  Upon inquiry on whether the money to be taken from the fund must be
paid back if a landfill is not placed on the Belvoir, Dan White, City Attorney, opined his belief that
the governing body can determine where to move funds within the budget, and that a b udget re-
appropriation as prepared b y the City Treasurer would reflect proposed changes to budget areas
pursuant to the Mayor’s reduction measures, the employee ordinances approved earlier in the meeting
that will involve changes to the budget, and whatever action is taken on the funding for the retirement
incentives.  He stated that without having specifically researched the subject, he is unaware of any
external statutory controls concerning the transfer of funds within the budget.  Upon further inquiry,
Ms. Nemecek briefly reviewed the environmental monitoring contract for the Happy Jack landfill
site.  D uring comments, Mr. Brown raised a point of order with Mayor Kaysen ruling comments
should rem ain germ ane t o t he s ubject o f t he res olution.  Di scussion f ollowed a nd i ncluded
information from Ms. Nemecek on expenses associated with the hauling of solid waste to Ault, CO
and proposed plans to increase the fund amount.  Topics discussed included:  Opposition to using
money set aside in the solid waste fund for retirement incentives; moving of funds to another account;
whether the funds utilized would be repaid to the fund; using solid waste fund monies for paying
retirement e xpenses; o ne-time funding; current budget c oncerns involving reduced r evenues a nd
probable less funding to be received through action of the state legislature; the need to reduce City
costs, and overall support for the employee voluntary early retirement incentives.  Dr. Rinne moved
to amend by changing t he source of funding from t he solid waste fund t o general fund reserves,
seconded by Mr. Collins.  Mr. Melko commented that the proposed amendment is a more acceptable
fund t o use, and John Palmer questioned borrowing t he money with t he intent of paying it back
instead o f j ust re- appropriating i t.  Upo n i nquiry, Deput y City T reasurer L ois Huf f adv ised an
estimated 30 day reserve amount is $4.2  m illion, and that t he City’s un-designated reserves are
currently at $4.4 million; designated and un-designated amount is $7.4 million.  Comments followed
on concerns with using unencumbered reserves when it is for emergency purposes, and borrowing
(loaning) the monies from the solid waste fund and replenishing it in the future.  Motion to amend
failed. Voting “yes” – Ms. Case, Mr. Collins and Dr. Rinne.  Voting “no” – Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles,
Mr. Pierson, Mr. Spiker, Mr. Valdez and Mayor Kaysen.  Upon inquiry, Human Resources Director
Rich Wiederspahn clarified an individual that retires under the state retirement system can be rehired
by the same employer in the future, however, there is a penalty to the employer as it must repay the
retiree’s share in full back into the system, which would affect the budget.  Motion carried.  Voting
“yes” - all members of the governing body present. (#5183)
[CA] “A RESOLUTION DE TERMINING THA T CO NDITIONS F OR A NNEXATION
REQUIRED BY WYO. STAT. §15-1-402 EXIST F OR THE PR OPERTY DESCRIBED AS A

2010, Finance Committee will meet at 11:00 a.m. and Public Services Committee will meet at 12:15
p.m. on Tuesday, February 16, 2010, in Council Meeting Room 122; Vouchers meeting will be held

officially adjourned at 6:23 p.m.

MEETING: City Council | DATE: 2010-02-22 | CLIP_ID: 142 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 142

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
February 22, 2010
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning
at 6 :00 p .m. i n C ouncil Chambers.  P resent w ere: M AYOR -  R ichard L.  K aysen; C OUNCIL
MEMBERS: Ms. Ash, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Ms. Case, Mr. Collins, Mr. Pierson, Dr. Rinne, Mr.
Spiker and Mr. Valdez.  Also present: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk; Barb Dorr, City Treasurer, and
Dan White, City Attorney.  The pledge of allegiance was recited.
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA]  are considered to be
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its
normal sequence on the agenda.)  Upon request by Dr. Rinne, and pursuant to support received by
Ms. Ash and Mr. Collins, agenda item #13 was removed from the Consent Agenda. Upon request
by Ms. Ash, and pursuant to support received by Dr. Rinne and Ms. Broyles, agenda item #16(c)
was removed from the Consent Agenda. Mr. Brown moved to approve remaining Consent Agenda
items, seconded by Mr. Spiker. Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on February 8, 2010.  Motion (per
Consent Agenda) carried.
[CA] Minutes from Vouchers Meeting of the Governing Body on February 16, 2010.  Motion (per
Consent Agenda) carried.
Mr. Spiker moved to approve payment of vouchers as presented, seconded by Mr. Brown.
Upon inquiry regarding a voucher payable to AVI, City Engineer Doug Vetter briefly reviewed the
status of the Henderson Basin project, stating there is some remaining work for remapping of the
flood plain prior to the project being completed.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of
the governing body.
Mayor Kaysen opened a public hearing for the purpose of receiving comments and input for
renewal of  e xisting City Liquor Licenses ( retail, re staurant, lim ited r etail, resort, b ar a nd g rill
licenses, and microbrewery and winery permits) filed with the City Clerk’s Office.  City Clerk Carol
Intlekofer noted for the record that one restaurant liquor licensee (Lucky 7) did not file for renewal,
and reviewed the parked license status for applicants C.B. & Potts, Hitching Post and On the Border.
She advised On the Border had submitted a written request for an additional one year extension, as
authorized by Wyo. Statute, to become operational, and stated that the Mayflower was temporarily
closed for  remode ling pur poses.  Chris Cantwell , Director of Ope rations for O n The B order
restaurant pro ject, reviewed the current status the projec t and explained, if the re quest for the
additional year is approved, it is anticipated building construction will be completed in Oct. 2010.
Upon inquiry, Captain J eff Schulz, I nterim Police Chief, re viewed underage compliance checks
recently conducted, responded to inquiry concerning non-compliance of TIPS training records, and
indicated he would provide underage compliance results pursuant to checks conducted last fall.
There being no further comments or input and purpose of the public hearing having been met,
Mayor Kaysen closed the hearing and referred the applications to Finance Committee.
“Ordinance annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, a parcel of land being situated in
Sections 12 and 13, Township 13 North, Range 67 West of the 6  Principal Meridian, Laramieth
County, Wyoming (located east of Southwest Drive, south of Interstate 80, west of BNSF Railroad,
and north of Woodenshoe  Drive) .” Donn Edmunds questioned w hether any  studies had bee n
conducted regarding accessibility to the site, expressing concerns with traffic delays and the railroad
crossing in the area.  Gay Woodhouse, Woodhouse Roden LLC law firm, attorney representing area
home owners, requested a postponement to fur ther review impact issues involving the proposed
annexation and a Southwest Drive  Transpor tation Draina ge and U tility repor t that had bee n
prepared. She cited previous postponements requested by and approved for the developer for the
project, submitted a handout (on file with office of City Clerk) noting the prior postponement dates,
and requested equal consideration pursua nt to her client’s r equest for postponement.  Kathlee n
Sheehan, property owner near the proposed annexation area, requested a six month postponement
to allow time to re-e valuate he r prope rty situation and review  the re port.  Ken Clar k,
applicant/developer, pro vided in formation o n th e dr ainage report, n oting it w as ma iled to  Ms.
Sheehan in Aug. 2009, with Ms. Sheehan responding she did not receive the report.  Reporting for
Public Services Committee, Ms. Ash moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Collins.
Ms. Ash moved to postpone for one month (to March 22, 2010), seconded by Mr. Collins.  Upon
inquiry, City Clerk Carol Intlekofer advised a one month postponement would be to March 22 , atnd
which time the item would be referred back to Public Services Committee for review.  Discussion
followed and included the following topics: Annexation request by the property owner, state statute
annexation criteria and g overning body’s role in dete rmining whether criteria is met, and wha t
would be accomplished within a 30 day postponement relative to the annexation action vs. other
issues involving site plan/platting discussions.  Upon inquiry, Ms. Woodhouse reiterated the request
for postponement is to provide conce rned area property owners the same c ourtesy afforded Mr.
Clark (i.e. pr evious postponement re quests approved) to allow additio nal time to further r eview
documents.  Ms. Case moved to amend the motion to postpone from one month to three  months,
seconded by Mr. Valdez.  Mike Basom, Dray, Thomson, Dyekman law firm, attorney representing
Mr. Clark, spoke in opposition to the motion to postpone and stated the issues of concern that have
been raised regarding access, transportation, utility, and drainage should be discussed during the
platting process.  Speaking in favor of postponement and against the annexation generally were Ms.
Sheehan and Doris Garcia, citing personal impact to property owners in the area if the annexation
and proposed development of the area as proceeds and expressing concerns with health, safety and
welfare of residents, and future residents, in the area.  Comments followed on the time frame (one
month increments) a nd purpose of past postponement reque sts made by  and appr oved for  the
developer, and included statements in support of the motion to postponement by Ms. Sheehan and
Ms. Woodhouse.  Motion to postpone for thre e months failed. Voting “yes” – Ms. Case and Mr.
Valdez.  Voting “no” – Ms. Ash, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Collins, Mr. Pierson, Dr. Rinne, Mr.
Spiker and May or Kaysen. Discussion con tinued on the f ormer Swan Ranch a nnexation action
recently approved that involved similar concerns with access and traffic, and information obtained
from Mr. Clark on his hesitancy to move forward with the expense of having an engineering report
prepared to address issues of concern involving access, traffic, and drainage topics without knowing
whether the annexation request will be approved.  Motion to postpone for one month (to March 22,
2010) carried.  V oting “yes” – Ms. B royles, Ms. Case, Mr. Collins, Dr . Rinne, Mr. Va ldez and
Mayor Kaysen. Voting “no” – Ms. Ash, Mr. Brown, Mr. Pierson and Mr. Spiker.
“Ordinance creating Chapter 8.68, Vacant and Abandoned Residential Structures, of Title
8, Health and Safety , of the Code of  the City  of Cheyenne, Wyoming.” Ian Cattalier, State of
Wyoming, Construction Mana gement, question ed the effect the ordinanc e may have on va cant
properties the State rec ently purcha sed (ne ar Saf eway store off  Pioneer Ave .); the aba ndoned
property definition, and the time fr ame for payment according to the fee structure for abandoned
properties.  Upon inquiry, Dan White, City Attorney, reviewed the basic aspects and provisions of
the ordinance in response to Mr. Cattalier’s inquiry.  Frank Cole, developer, spoke in opposition to
ordinance provisions involving specific requirements on boarding up the structure s, and stated a
better definition of “utilities” vs. provided “services” should be provided.  Steven Miller spoke in
favor of the ordinance and in support of holding property owners accountable, cleaning up blighted
and a bandoned r esidential pr operties, a nd e ffect on  the  ne ighborhood pr operty va lues.  Jea nie
Tucker questioned whether there was any protection for abandoned homes located in a designated
historic district and the fees to be assessed and also read a statement from Jan Nelson Schroll in
support of preserving and promoting neighborhoods and registering abandoned houses. Reporting
for Finance Committee, Mr. B rown stated the re was no re commendation on the item fr om the
committee.  Ms. Ash moved to appr ove on second reading, seconded by Ms. Broyles.  Dr. Rinne
moved to amend Section 8.68.070 B.1. and B.2. to delete the language following the word “secured”
as follows: B.1. “ by the use of Phillip s head two-inch screws” and B.2. “by the use of 16 pe nny
common nails”, seconded by Mr. Collins.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” – all members
of the governing body.  U pon inquiry regarding #6 in S ection 8.68.040, Mr. White advised this
wording was included to obtain information regarding plans or reasons why a particular structure
has bee n abandone d, and Of ficer Emmett L usher, Code Enf orcement, Ch eyenne Poli ce D ept.,
advised any information that can be utilized to quickly assist in identifying property ownership is
useful in abating properties.  Officer Lusher also advised it is important to board up properties to
protect them from crime and illegal activities, indicating every City Ward has issues with abandoned
properties.  Comments followed reg arding the diffe rence between utilities and service s.  Upon
inquiry, Mr. White advised the  International Property Maintenance Code (IPMC) only applies to
dangerous or  unsafe  structure s that are  capa ble of collapse, and structur es unfit for huma n
occupancy and explained it is unlawful to sell property that is in violation of the IPMC unless the
new owner acknowledges and agrees to be res ponsible for violations.  Upon furthe r inquiry, Mr.
White advised the ordinance supplements the IPMC, provides a financial incentive to p roperty
owners to maintain their proper ties, provides funds to assist with monit oring the prope rties by
building officials and police , and plac es restrictions on the building to allow f or an inspec tion
process.  Discussion followed on the purpose of the ordinance and possible future similar ordinance
to ad dress abandoned commercial buildings.  U pon inquiry regarding the $250 fee, Mr. White
clarified once the abandoned building is registered and the fee paid, the building official would have
a list of abandoned/vacated properties for the police and building official to monitor.  Upon inquiry,
Mayor Kaysen advised the authority to monitor ordinance pr ovisions would fall un der the Chief
Building Official’s area with enforcement through Nuisance Control and/or Police Dept.  Discussion
continued on other potential changes to ordinance wording, compliance time frame and penalty
provisions.  Mr. Cole provided further comments on the difference between  utilities and services.
Upon inquiry regarding issuance of misdemeanor citations, Mr. White reviewed the role of the Chief
Building Official and police department relative to issuance of citations.  Comments followed on
uninhabitable structures, similar legislation for abandoned commercial structures, and the terms
“utility” vs. “services”.  Motion to approve as amended on second reading carried.  Voting “yes”
– all members of the governing body.
“An Ordinance annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, a parcel of land being situated
in the SW ¼ NW¼ of Section 21, T. 14N., R .66W., of the 6  P.M., Laramie County, Wyomingth
(located south of Plain View Road, west of Point Bluff, and north of Apache Street) -- referred to
Public Services Committee (sponsor - Ms. Ash).
[CA] “Resolution certifying that the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne is in agreement with
the Laramie County Board of Commissioners as to the current countywide Consensus Block Grant
application to t he State L oan and I nvestment Boa rd.” Motion (pe r Consent Ag enda) car ried.
(#5185)
“Resolution authorizing the transfer of funds in the approximate amount of $145,000 from
the City’s self-funded employee health benefit plan to the general fund.”  Mr . Brown moved to
adopt, seconded by Mr. Spiker.   Dr. Rinne moved to amend the  6th Whereas pa ragraph and the
Now, The refore, B e It Resol ved paragraph to delete the f ollowing language at the e nd of the
sentences: “to re duce the  number of pote ntial u npaid employee f urlough day s which may  be
imposed due to current budgetary concerns and conditions,” seconded by Mr. Collins.  Comments
followed on the purpose of the proposed amendment, which is to utilize the dollars toward funding
of job s in  lie u o f a ny po tential r eduction in  e mployee fo rce measures in stead o f s pecifically
referencing unpaid employee furlough days.  Mayor Kaysen stated the monies are a one-time lump
sum amount and not an ongoing revenue source, and that the intent of the resolution is to hopefully
defer/reduce the need for furlough days.  Upon inquiry, City Treasurer Barb Dorr confirmed the
money is a one-time infusion of money.  Brief discussion followed on the purpose of the proposed
amendment, and time frame of funding transfer (current or next fiscal year).  Motion to amend
failed.  Voting “yes” – Ms. Ash and Dr. Rinne.  Voting “no” – Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Ms. Case,
Mr. Collins, M r. Pierson, Mr. Spiker, Mr. V aldez and May or Kaysen.  Discussion followed on
possible unpaid employee furlough days, concern with protection of employee jobs, retaining the
funding relative to re-implementation of a possible future self-funded program as a health insurance
option, and fiscal responsibility.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” –  Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr.
Collins, Mr. Pierson, Mr. Spiker, Mr. Valdez and Mayor Kaysen. Voting “no” - Ms. Ash, Ms. Case
and Dr. Rinne.  (#5186)
[CA] “Resolution re-appropriating the Fiscal Year 2010 budget of the City of Cheyenne according
to the provisions of W.S. §16-4-112 and §16-4-113” -- referred to Finance Committee (sponsors -
Mr. Brown and Mr. Spiker).
[CA] “Resolution designating the Chey enne Animal Shelter, 800 Southwest Drive, Chey enne,
Wyoming, as an official animal shelter and animal control facility pursuant to Section 6.20.010 of
the Code of the City of Cheyenne” -- referred to Public Services Committee (sponsor - Ms. Broyles).
[CA] Notice and Consent to Assignment of the Juvenile Housing agreement between the City of
Cheyenne and Cornerstone Programs Corporation dba Jeffrey C. Wardle Academy to Southeastern
Wyoming Juvenile Services, LLC.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried.  (#5460)
[CA] Community Based Family Support and Preservation Grant contract between the Wyoming
Department o f F amily Se rvices, Pr otective Se rvices D ivision, an d Ci ty of  Che yenne, Y outh
Alternatives, to provide a family counselor to assist with transitional services.  Motion (per Consent
Agenda) carried.  (#5514)
Wyoming Children’ s Trust Fu nd Gra nt contrac t betwee n the Wy oming De partment of
Family Services, Protective Services Division, and City of Cheyenne, Special Friends - Y outh
Alternatives, for the Special Friends Outreach Program.  Ms. Ash declared a conflict of interest
(participant o n th e Tr ust F und B oard) a nd va cated Cou ncil Ch ambers.  M r. B rown mov ed to
approve, seconded by Mr. Valdez.  Motion carried. Voting “yes” – all members of the governing
body present in City Council Chambers.  Following announcement of the vote, Ms. Ash returned
to Council Chambers.  (#5515)
[CA] Energy Perfor mance contra ct betwe en the City  of Chey enne and L ong Building
Technologies, Inc. to provide e quipment and services designed to save energy at 52 City  owned
facilities to include a  lighting retrofit, occupancy sensor installation, modular boiler in Fire Station
#1, an energy conservation education program and measurement and verification of energy savings.
Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried.  (#5516)
 [CA] Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and the Wyoming Department of State Parks &
Cultural Resources for a Land and Water Conservation Fund Grant for a basketball court in Martin
Luther King Park.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (City’s portion- cash $9,597 and in-kind
match $9,416).  (#5517)
[CA] Lease Agreement between the City of Cheyenne, acting by and through its Board of Public
Utilities (BOPU), and Swan Ranch Development for Swan Ranch Development to construct and to
donate to BOPU a private water and sanitary sewer system that runs through the Swan Ranch Rail
Park -- referred to Finance Committee.    
[CA] Memorandum of Understanding between the City of Cheyenne, acting by and through its
Board of Public Utilities (BOPU), and Cheyenne Frontier Days, to outline responsibilities for the
operation and maintenance of the sanitary waste station at Lake Absarraca -- referred to Finance
Committee. 
[CA] Consideration of FAA Grant No. 3-56-0005-45 between the City of Cheyenne, County of
Laramie, and the Cheyenne Regional Airport Board for the Rehabilitation of Taxiways A3 and B2 --
referred to Finance Committee. 
[CA] Amendment No. 4 to State Lease Agreement No. 8-09466 between the City of Cheyenne and
State of Wyoming for parking spaces in the Cox Parking Facility -- referred to Finance Committee.
[CA] Consideration of Bid #R-8-10 for the Surplus Sale of “Two 1993 Toro Greens Aerators”
from the City of Cheyenne Golf Department.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (Mountain Vista
Greens, Ft. Collins, CO (one aerator), revenue to City  $1,200.00).
[CA] Consideration of Bid #M-13-10 for a contract between S&S Builders, of Cheyenne, WY, and
the City of Cheyenne for the Randa ll Bridge Enhancements Project (2007-2010 1% Sales Tax).
Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (rejected all bids).
Under other City  business:  J ohn Palmer expressed c oncerns with the g overning body’s
public participation process, commented on a recent editorial in the local newspaper written by a
member of City Council, and the city’s budget condition and costs associated with city services and
maintenance areas.  Joe E lkins, BC Cab, local business owner, expressed concerns with public
transport taxicab companies operating in violation of state law, referencing City Code provisions,
and lack of enforcement for the violations, which would provide revenue to the City to assist with
its financial situation.  Pacomio Sandoval also expressed co ncerns with the public participa tion
process at City Council meetings and with budget concerns and expenses, specifically referencing
the City ’s pas t Taco John’s Events Center  and B elvoir Ranch pur chases.  Donn Edmunds
commented on the budget situation and referenced a community in Colorado that is experiencing
the financial inability to provide support services involving urban growth (annexation) areas, and
urged the governing body to carefully consider housing development growth/annexation approval
actions in conjunction with the City’s financial ability to support affiliated services.
Mr. Collins made statements expressing his opinion regarding a recent executive session of
the governing body and allowable discussion topics.
City Attorney Dan White requested the governing body meet in executive session concerning
a pleading/filing involving litigation.  Ms. Broyles moved to meet in executive session, seconded
by Mr. Pierson.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.  Mayor Kaysen
announced members would meet in executive session in Council Chambers, not open to the public;
requested the City Attorney, City Clerk, and other staff as requested by the City Attorney, be present
at the executive session, and stated there would be no action taken by the governing body as a result
of the executive session, and that the regular City Council meeting would officially conclude at the
close of the executive session.  (Clerk’s Note: Executive session convened at 8:16 p.m.)
There being  no further  busine ss to come be fore the g overning body prior to executive
session, the meeting was officially adjourned at 8:34 p.m. following closure of the executive session.
 
 Submitted by,
 
 
 
 Carol Intlekofer, MMC
 City Clerk

The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning

Absent:  M s. Case and Mr. Collins.  Also present: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk; Barb Dorr, City
Treasurer, and Dan White, City Attorney.  The pledge of allegiance was recited.
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA]  are considered to be
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two

Mr. Brown moved to approve payment of vouchers as presented, seconded by Mr. Spiker.
Upon inquiry, City Treasurer Barb Dorr provided information available for vouchers payable to

($1,111.31/$1,402.31), NAARSO carnival ride training expenses for building inspectors (reimbursed

“Ordinance creating Chapter 8.68, Vacant and Abandoned Residential Structures, of Title
8, Health and Safety, o f the Code o f t he City o f Cheyenne, Wyoming.” Reporting for Finance
Committee, Mr. Brown moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Valdez. Mr.
Brown moved to amend by substitute, seconded by Mr. Valdez.  It was noted the substitute amends
Section 8.68.040 B. by changing “7 days” to “7 business days”.  Motion to amend carried. Voting
“yes” – all members of the governing body present.  Mr. Spiker moved to amend the substitute, as
amended, by a replacement substitute (dated by City Attorney as March 8, 2010), seconded by Mr.
Valdez.  Upon inquiry, City Attorney Dan White explained the changes contained in the substitute,
referencing Section 8.68.020, Definitions, Subsection 4. with regard to c onnection to u tilities or
services and definition of “abandoned”; Section 8.68.110 pertaining to structure registration and
property owner duty to comply with the International Property Maintenance Code, and insertion of
Section 3. providing for a sunset clause indicating the ordinance shall remain in effect from May 1,
2010 u ntil Au gust 1 , 2 011 w ith a  r eport r equirement f rom t he C hief B uilding O fficial to t he
governing body prior to August 1, 2011 to determine ordinance effectiveness.  Motion to amend the
substitute, a s a mended, by r eplacement s ubstitute c arried.  Voting “yes” –  a ll members o f t he
governing body present.  Mr. Pierson moved to amend Section 3. (sunset clause) by changing the
date of August 1, 2011 to November 1, 2011, seconded by Mr. Spiker.  He noted the proposed date
change would  provide more time for acquiring information to determine ordinance effectiveness.
Comments followed in support of the amendment.  Motion to amend Section 3. carried.  Voting
“yes” – all members of the governing body present.  Dr. Rinne moved to amend Section 8.68.020
by s triking Subs ection 4.  c. wh ich references t he C ity’s wat er supply s ystem.  F ollowing b rief
comments, Dr. Rinne withdrew the motion.  Upon inquiry regarding enforcement of the ordinance,
Matt Ashby, Planning Se rvices D irector, a dvised u pon a pproval of the o rdinance a  p reliminary
process will be developed regarding structure registration and enforcement procedures involving the
Chief Building Official with assistance from the Nuisance Code Enforcement Officer.  Main motion
as amended by substitute (three amendments) carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing
body present. (#3884)

the meeting, together with any motions and votes.  She noted, pursuant to state statute requirements,
publication o f t he n otice o f ren ewal appl ications an d po sting o f n otices o n respe ctive l icensee
premises have been achieved and the holding of a public hearing before the governing body has been
conducted.  She further stated that the City Attorney has advised that this statement on the record
provides s ufficient d ocumentation t o identify t hose individual r enewal a pplications t hat may be
acted upon at the meeting.  Upon query by Mayor Kaysen to members of the governing body, there
was no objection voiced to recognize the renewal applicants as listed on the agenda in lieu of the
Clerk r eading t he individual applicants.  Mr.  Br own moved t o app rove t he r enewal app licants,
seconded by Mr. Valdez.  Mr. Brown moved to divide the question to allow for a separate vote on
the renewal application filed by Sandra J. I nniss d/b/a 2 Do ors Down, seconded by Mr. Valdez.
Motion to divide the question carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.
Mr. Brown moved to approve the renewal application filed by Sandra J. Inniss d/b/a 2 Doors Down,
seconded by Mr. Valdez.  Mr. Spiker declared a conflict of interest (business interest) and vacated
Council Chambers.   Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present in
Council Chambers.  Following announcement of the vote, Mr. Spiker returned to Council Chambers.
Mr. Brown moved to divide the question to allow for a separate vote on those renewal applicants
who received 2 or more failures during the Police Department’s last 2 compliance checks and also
for the renewal application for Bedrock Seafood, Inc. d/b/a Mayflower Restaurant, seconded by Mr.
Pierson. Motion to divide the question carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body
present.   Mr. Brown moved to approve the renewal applications as divided in the previous motion
that carried, seconded by Mr. Spiker.  Mr. Brown stated that following discussion and conclusion
of last week’s Finance Committee meeting, information had been received from Capt. Schulz, Police
Dept., th at th ere w ere no renewal a pplicants th at r eceived 2  or  more failures d uring th e last 2
compliance checks,  t herefore, t he Mayflower Restaurant renewal application was t he o nly o ne
involved in the motion on the floor.  John Parker, owner of Mayflower Restaurant, reviewed the
current status (minor remodeling, new management, re-opening lounge only without food services
at this time) and future plans involving restaurant o perations.  C omments followed in support of
renewing t he l icense, di sturbances an d call s for police ser vices, co mpliance ch ecks, an d p ast
management co ncerns.  Upo n i nquiry, Po lice C hief Bri an K ozak rev iewed depart ment reco rds
research co ncerning t he number o f calls for service and repo rting part ies.  Comment was made
referencing a statement made by an applicant representative when the retail liquor license was before
City Council to approve a transfer of ownership to the current licensee (re: utilization of a bar and
grill liquor license instead of a retail liquor license).  Mr. Parker advised the statement was made by
a previous representative, who was no longer with the business, and clarified the facility does need
a full retail liquor license to operate as a lounge environment without restaurant (food) services.
Motion to approve the renewal application for Bedrock Seafood, Inc. d/b/a Mayflower Restaurant
carried. Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present. Comments followed regarding
the ne ed t o c ontinue p romotion o f p olice c ompliance c hecks a nd TIPS ( alcohol s erver a nd
employee) training.  M ain motion to approve remaining renewal applications (en masse) carried.
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.
Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held March 22, 2010 at 6:00 p.m. in City
Council Chambers for a restaurant liquor license application filed with the City Clerk’s Office for
Maria Ramos d/b/a El Charrito Mexican Grill, 216 E 17  Street, Cheyenne, WY.th
Announcement was made of a Public Meeting to be held March 22, 2010 at 6:00 p.m. in City
Council Chambers for t he purpo se o f amending Re solution No . 4859 pert aining t o 2007-2010
Optional One Percent Sales Tax Funding due to a projected shortfall in collections.
Announcement was made of the Appointment of Lane Pilbin to the International Fire Code
Board of Appeals; Terry Livingston to the Greenway Advisory Committee; Cindy Pomeroy to the
Police Civil Service Commission; and Rita Watson and Rick Schum to the Personnel Commission,
to be confirmed March 22, 2010.
Under other City business:
Joe Elkins, Better Check Cab, local business owner, commended the Mayor and City Council
members on the recent hiring of Police Chief Brian Kozak, and expressed concerns with other public
transport t axicab co mpanies o perating i n v iolation o f s tate l aw, al so ref erencing Ci ty Co de
provisions, and lack of violation enforcement, which would provide revenue to the City to assist
with its financial situation. Mayor Kaysen advised a new ordinance is in draft form regarding public
transport companies. 
Mike Langhoff questioned guidelines for tournament-style poker events and enforcement
of g ames he  be lieved t o be  i llegal, r eferencing a dvertising f lyers he had a cquired r egarding
upcoming p oker e vents.  C omments f ollowed r egarding g ames being c onducted th at a re n ot
following the City’s recently enacted ordinance.  Upon inquiry, City Clerk Carol Intlekofer clarified
one permit had been issued since the ordinance went into effect and when individuals apply for a
tournament-style poker event permit in addition to the application form, they are also given a copy
of the ordinance and affiliated administrative guidelines. Comments continued on the administrative
guidelines.  Upon inquiry, City Attorney Dan White advised he would proceed with adopting the
administrative g uidelines u nder p rovisions o f t he Wy oming Ad ministrative P rocedure Ac t a nd
would communicate with Police Kozak to discuss the means of distribution of information to those
who may be affected.
Mr. Valdez commented on complaints he had received regarding pot holes throughout town.
Upon inquiry, Jim Elias, Public Works Director, advised the formation of pot holes are an on-going
problem, exacerbated this winter by freezing/thawing conditions. He requested citizens report pot
holes requiring repair through the on-line (City’s website) Request Partner reporting system or by
phoning t he h ot line n umber a t 6 37-6263.  M ayor K aysen c ommented that o nly a  c old mix
temporary repair can be utilized during winter months and when warmer weather arrives a more
lasting patch can be applied, and expressed appreciation to the public for their patience.
Mayor Kaysen recognized youth members of the community in attendance at the meeting.
There b eing n o f urther b usiness t o come b efore t he go verning b ody, t he m eeting was
officially adjourned at 7:08 p.m. 
 
 Submitted by,
 
 
 
 Carol Intlekofer, MMC
 City Clerk
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request.
 
Published: Wyoming Tribune-Eagle
 March 19, 2010

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
March 22, 2010
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning
at 6 :00 p .m. i n C ouncil C hambers.  Pr esent w ere: M AYOR - Richard L . K aysen; C OUNCIL
MEMBERS: Ms. Ash, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Collins, Mr. Spiker and Mr. Valdez.  Absent:
Ms. Case, Mr. Pierson and Dr. Rinne.  Also present: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk; Barb Dorr, City
Treasurer, and Dan White, City Attorney.  Mayor Kaysen recognized members of Boy Scout Troop
216 who led the pledge of allegiance.
Ms. B royles r ecognized L isa S cholz, E xecutive D irector, Cheyenne-Laramie C ounty
Methamphetamine Initiative and expressed appreciation to the Meth Initiative Committee members.
She pro vided a b rief hi story o f t he es tablishment o f t he m eth i nitiative, h ow t he pro gram h as
benefitted the community through the Meth Resource Center and specifically noted Ms. Scholz’
efforts in educating the public on the dangers of using methamphetamine. Ms. Broyles advised the
meth initiative program will continue on a voluntary basis by interested members of the community.
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA]  are considered to be
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its
normal sequence on the agenda.)  Upon request by Ms. Ash, and pursuant to support received by Mr.
Collins and Mr. Spiker, agenda item #34(a) was removed from the Consent Agenda. Mr. Collins
moved t o appro ve remaining Co nsent Agenda items, seconded by Mr. Bro wn. Motion carried.
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on March 8, 2010.  M otion (per
Consent Agenda) carried.
[CA] Minutes from Vouchers Meeting of the Governing Body on March 15, 2010.  Motion (per
Consent Agenda) carried.
Mr. Brown moved to approve payment of vouchers as presented, seconded by Mr. Spiker.
Upon i nquiry, C ity Cl erk C arol In tlekofer e xplained s everal b usiness l icense f ee re funds w ere
included in the voucher listing involving overpayments.  Upon further inquiry, City Treasurer Barb
Dorr, Parks and Recreation Asst. Director Todd Feezer and Engineering Services Director Nathan
Beauheim provided information available for a voucher payable to BOPU ($2,140.88) for water tap
fees at the David Romero Youth Activity Center. Mr. Spiker moved to remove the BOPU voucher
for payment until further clarification could be provided, seconded by Mr. Collins.  Motion carried.
Voting “yes” - al l members o f t he go verning body present.  Upon inquiry regarding a v oucher
payable t o Meridan Fire & Security ($805), Fire Chief Guy Cameron advised services involved
repair of the fire system panel located at the Fire Training Facility.  Motion to approve remaining
vouchers carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.
Mayor Kaysen opened a public hearing to receive input and comments for a restaurant liquor
license application filed with the City Clerk’s Office for Maria Ramos d/b/a El Charrito Mexican
Grill, 216 E 17  Street, Cheyenne, WY.  T here being no comments and purpose of the hearingth
having been met, Mayor Kaysen closed the hearing and referred the item to Finance Committee.
Mayor Kaysen opened a public meeting to receive input and comments for the purpose of
amending Resolution No. 4859 pertaining to 2007-2010 Optional One Percent Sales Tax Funding
due t o a pr ojected shortfall in co llections.  Vicki Nemecek, A ssistant Director, P ublic Works,
advised a n anticipated r evenue shortfall o f 5  p enny sales t ax has necessitated a review o f t heth
recommended d istribution o f fu nds a nd r eported s ales t ax c ollections w ere e stimated t o fa ll
$4,999,500 short of the projected $45,000,000 original estimate.  She reviewed the status of projects
and proposed re-allocation of funds in various areas.  Mayor Kaysen advised an affiliated agenda
item (Resolution Amending Resolution No. 4859) would be reviewed at the Monday, April 5, 2010
(11:00 a.m.) Finance Committee meeting.  Frank Cole spoke in opposition to reduction in street
maintenance and construction (repair) projects, particularly regarding snow removal.  Connie Sloan-
Cathcart, Director, United Way of Laramie County, clarified 23 human resources agencies will be
affected by t he p roposed c uts in funding. There being no  further c omments o r d iscussion a nd
purpose of the meeting having been met, Mayor Kaysen closed the public meeting.

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
April 12, 2010
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning
at 6 :00 p .m. i n C ouncil Chambers.  P resent w ere: M AYOR -  R ichard L.  K aysen; C OUNCIL
MEMBERS: Ms. Ash, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Collins, Mr. Pierson, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Spiker
and Mr. Valdez.  Absent:  Ms. Case.  Also present: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk; Barb Dorr, City
Treasurer, and Dan White, City Attorney.  The pledge of allegiance was recited.
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA]  are considered to be
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its
normal sequence on the agenda.)  Upon request by Mr. Collins, and pursuant to support received by
Mr. Valdez and Dr. Rinne, agenda items #15 and 16 were removed from the Consent Agenda. Upon
request by Mr. Brown, and pursuant to support received by Mr. Collins and Ms. Ash, agenda item
#20 was re moved from the Consent Ag enda. Mr. Brown moved to appr ove remaining Consent
Agenda items, sec onded by  M r. Valdez. Motion carr ied. Voting  “y es” – a ll members of the
governing body present.
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on March 22, 2010.  Motion (per
Consent Agenda) carried.
[CA] Minutes from Vouche rs Meeting of the Gover ning Body on April 5, 2010.  Motion (per
Consent Agenda) carried.
Ms. Ash moved to approve payment of vouchers as presented, seconded by Ms. Broyles. Mr.
Collins and Mr. Valdez each declared a conflict of interest (business interest in vouchers payable
to the Bicycle Station and Radio Station KYOY respectively) and vacated Council Chambers. Upon
inquiry, City Treasurer Barb Dorr, provided information available for vouchers payable to Dex West
LLC ($140.25), advertising in yellow pages for Civic Center and Sanitation Department, and Accent
Plumbing and He ating ($149.90), De pot building re stroom repa irs, and Cintas Fire  Protection
($323.30), annual inspection of kitchen system, hood, etc. in Depot building.  The City Attorney was
requested to revie w the lea se agreement regarding City vs. tenant De pot building maintenanc e
responsibilities.  Mo tion carried.  Vo ting “yes” – all members of the governing body present in
Council Chambers.  Following announcement of the vote, Mr. Collins and Mr. Valdez returned to
Council Chambers. 
“An Ordinance amending Title 17, Zoning, Chapter 17.08, Zoning Districts Established and
Section 17.08.040 Chey enne City  map of the Chey enne City  Code, by  chang ing the  zoning
classification from MR-1 Medium Density Residential Established District to PUD Planned Unit
Development District for Lot 1, Orig inal City Block 140, Refinement A, Che yenne, Wyoming
(located at the nor thwest corn er of  the intersec tion of  E. 24  Street and Warr en Ave nue).”th
Reporting for Public Services Committee, Ms. Ash moved to approve on third and final reading,
seconded by Mr. Collins.  Ms. Ash moved to amend by substitute, seconded by Dr. Rinne.  Upon
inquiry, L isa Paffor d, Building  and De velopment offic e, ad vised the substitute includes
incorporation of pre vious staff re commendation #1 re garding landscape setbacks, and pr ovides
language regarding neighborhood architectural character considerations, size limitation for food
service area, additional landscaping and parking requirements, and elimination of freestanding signs.
Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.  Comments
followed regarding the planting of replacement trees, pursuant to the orig inal street tree planting
system, incorporated into the PUD in a n effort to retain the area’s historic tree character.  Ma in
motion as amended by substitute carried. Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.
(#3886)
“An Ordinance amending a portion of the City of Cheyenne’s Sanitation Fee Schedule to add
monthly rec ycle du mpster se rvice fee s for a partments, multiplex  dwelling s, trailer c ourts and
optional commercial container service fees.” Upon inquiry by Ed Strader, Dennis Pino, Solid Waste
Director, provided information on the proposed f ee sc hedule, whic h is based upon the
number/frequency of pickups.  Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. Brown moved to approve on
third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Valdez. Upon inquiry, Mr. Pino advised there is currently
one container placed at duplexes, clarified business recycling is currently voluntary, and explained
that apartments fall under the residential, not commercial, fee category.  Motion carried.  Voting
“yes” – all members of the governing body present. (#3887)
“An Ordinance amending Chapter 5.12, Alcoholic Beverages, Section 5.12.060, Sales and
Consumption - Restrictions, of Title 5, Business Licenses and Regulations, of the Cheyenne City
Code pertaining to the David R. Romero South Cheyenne Community Park.”  Mr. Brown moved
to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Valdez.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all
members of the governing body present.   (#3888)
“An Ordinance annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, a parcel of land being situated
in Sections 12 and 13, Township 13 North, Range 67 West of the 6  Principal Meridian, Laramieth
County, Wyoming (located East of Southwest Drive, South of Interstate 80, West of BNSF Railroad,
and North of Woodenshoe Drive).”  Kathleen Sheehan, area property owner, requested consideration
of a postponement to further research possible removal of Woodenshoe Drive from the proposed
annexation property, and to allow her additional time to evaluate potential future monetary liability
involving deve lopment cos ts.  Providing c omments in support of Ms. Sheehan’s r equest for
postponement were Dan Frank, Frank Law Office, attorney representing Ms. Sheehan, who also
reviewed boundaries of Ms. Sheehan’s property relative to affiliated potential development costs
due to annexation; Gay Woodhouse, attorney representing residents along Southwest Drive, and Leo
Garcia.  Topics presented included the City’s previous Swan Ranch annexation action currently in
litigation, contiguity issues, and prior postponements requested by and approved for the developer
of the proje ct.  Mike B asom, Dray, Thomson and Dy ekman law firm, attorney representing the
developer, spoke in opposition to postponement, noting previous postponements requested by the
developer were to address concerns expressed by residents in the area.  Mr. Basom requested the
annexation process be allowed to move f orward, and commented that any action on the previous
Swan Ranch annexation would be handle d through a court decision.  Ms. Sheehan indica ted her
belief that Woodenshoe Drive did not necessarily need to be included in the annexation area.  Ms.
Woodhouse noted previous postponements approved for the developer, noting a past postponement
was for the developer to seek permission from the railroad.  Ms. Ash moved to approve on second
reading, seconded by Mr. Collins. Upon inqui ry regarding what communica tion or actions had
transpired between the developer and property owners since the last postponement wa s granted,
Brad Emmons, AVI, agent for applicant, clarified they had met with Ms. Sheehan r egarding the
annexation area and criteria to be designed through the platting process, and indicated until the plat
design is completed it is unknown whether Woodenshoe Drive should be included.  He stated they
have been working with City, County, WYDOT and BOPU staff on various issues.  Ms. Sheehan
verified she had met with the developer and certain staff involved and still has concerns regarding
several planning/development areas that could affect potential development of her property and the
financial burden annexation may cause her.  Ms. Woodhouse advised she has been meeting with her
clients as well as conducting other research during the most recent postponement time frame.  Ken
Clark, Gar rett Proper ties, develope r, advised h e started mee ting with landowne rs last summer
regarding the  pr oposed a nnexation a nd de velopment p roject to address i ssues, a nd sp oke in
opposition to postponement.  Comments followed regarding previously raised concerns with area
traffic, drainage, water and sewer, etc., which issues may not be able to be  completely addressed
until the platting process is started (i.e. after annexation), and cost to the developer to proceed with
the platting design process without knowing whether the annexation will be approved. Upon inquiry
regarding the inclusion of Woodenshoe Drive in the annexation area, Matt Ashby, Planning Services
Director, advised the key to including Woodenshoe Drive involves a long standing policy that has
been developed to assist with mitigating development impact on adjacent rights-of-way.  He noted
if changes were to be made to the annexation map by removing Woodenshoe Drive, “402 criteria”
would need to be re-evaluated.  Upon inquiry regarding measures taken to resolve Ms. Sheehan’s
concerns, Mr. Clark advised he has met with Ms. Sheehan and told her they would work with her
and others as the deve lopment moves forward during the plattin g process to seek solutions. M r.
Valdez moved to postpone for one month, seconded by Ms. Broyles.  Motion to postpone failed.
Voting “yes” – Mr. Valdez.  Voting “no” –  Ms. Ash, Mr . Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Collins, Mr.
Pierson, Dr. Rin ne, Mr. Spiker and  Mayor Kaysen.  Ma in motion carried.  Vo ting “yes” – a ll
members of the governing body present with the exception of Mr. Valdez voting “no”.
“An Ordinance amending Title 17, Zoning, Chapter 17.08, Zoning Districts Established and
Section 17.08.040 C heyenne City  map of the Chey enne City  Code, by  chang ing the  zoning
classification from PUD Planned Unit Development District to MR-2 Medium Density Residential-
Developing District for L ots 1 through 14 inc lusive of Dry  Creek T win Homes Subdivision,
Cheyenne, Wyoming, to be known as a portion of Woodhaven Subdivision (located west of College
Drive, be tween the  Dry Creek  Channel and McCann A venue)” --  ref erred to Public Services
Committee (sponsor - Ms. Ash).
“An Ordinance amending Title 17, Zoning, Chapter 17.08, Zoning Districts Established and
Section 17.08.040 Chey enne City  map of the Chey enne City  Code, by  chang ing the  zoning
classification from MR-1 Medium Density Residential-Established District to MUB Mixed Use with
Business Emphasis District for L ots 1 and 2 ; and the north 66-feet of Lots 3 and 4, Bloc k 195,
Original City, Cheyenne, Wyoming (located at the southwest corner of the intersection of E. 23rd
Street and Russell Avenue)” -- referred to Public Services Committee (sponsor - Ms. Ash).
[CA] “A Resolution authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a Final Plat for Original City
Block 140, Refinement A, a  replat of L ots 1-8, Block 140, Orig inal City, Cheyenne, Wyoming
(located at the northwest corner of the intersection of E. 24th Street and Warren Avenue).”  Motion
(per Consent Agenda) carried.  (#5198)
“A Resolution amending Resolution #4859 due to a shortfall in projected collection of the
Optional One Percent Sales Tax for calendar years 2007 through 2010.”  Mr. Brown moved to adopt,
seconded by Mr. Valdez.  Upon inquiry, Vicki Nemecek, Public Works Dept., provided information
on reduced level of funding percentages for certain areas and agencies affected, and clarified the
dollar amount proposed to be reduced for economic development.  Discussion followed on reduced
funding for economic development purposes; funding for the City’s LEADS membership, and other
available funding to assist with possible infrastructure requirements related to businesses that may
be intere sted in building in Chey enne.  Ms. Ne mecek provided in formation on infrastruc ture
development funding and explained the difference between budget line items for infr astructure
development vs. economic development and allowable use of each fund.  Concern was voiced with
reducing the funding level for economic development.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members
of the governing body present. (#5199)
“A Resolution authorizing the transfer of funds in the approximate amount of $400,000.00
from the City’s Solid Waste Management Fund to the General Fund.” Eric Hardgrave, Cheyenne
Public Employees Association (CPEA) President, spoke in favor of the resolution and intent of the
resolution.  Mr. Brown moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. Valdez.  Concerns were expressed that
transferring the funds into the general fund at this time may be premature based on the current fiscal
year (FY) 2010 budget and upcoming discussion on the FY2011 budget to be held at a Council work
session scheduled for April 22.  Mr. Brown moved to postpone for two weeks (to April 26, 2010)
to allow for discussion to be he ld involving the resolution at the Counci l’s budget work session,
seconded by Mr. Collins.  U pon inquiry, City Treasurer Barb Dorr stated, based upon the last
analysis of the current budget, it appears revenues and expenditures may balance out for the FY2010
budget but it is not absolutely certain yet whether the funds may be needed for the current fiscal
year, noting the f unds, or a  portion thereof, may be needed to balance next year’s budget which
currently reflects a $1.4 million shortfall.  Discussion followed and included the following topics:
Governing body’s fiscal responsibility, postponement of the resolution to allow for information on
next year’s budget to be received at the work session, intent of the resolution, which is to eliminate
possible employee furlough days for the remainder of the current budget year, need to utilize the
funding for either the current and/or next fiscal year, and suggested use of City reserves vs. funds
from the Solid Waste Management fund. Ms. Dorr clarified the $400,000.00 will be included in
discussion at the Council’s budg et work se ssion with May or Kaysen providing  additional
information of funding topics that will be discussed at the work session.  Discussion continued.  Ms.
Dorr provided additional information regarding level of revenues being received and, upon inquiry,
advised the estimated level of City reserves at the end of FY2010, if revenues and expenditures are
even, is $6.75 million of which $4.4 million is available for use (less than a 30 day reserve) with the
remaining funds being encumbered.  Motion to postpone carried.  Voting “yes” – Mr. Brown, Ms.
Broyles, Mr. Collins, Mr. S piker and Mayor Kaysen.  Voting  “no” – Ms. Ash, Mr . Pierson, Dr.
Rinne and Mr. Valdez.
“A Resolution amending Resolution No. 2852 establishing a payment schedule for Stage II
of the  Che yenne Wat er P roject.”  Ed  Str ader s poke in favor o f t he re solution wh ich w ould
temporarily suspend the City’s annual payment to the Board of Public Utilities (BOPU).  Er ic
Hardgrave, CPEA President, spoke in favor of the resolution explaining suspension of the payment
will help save employee jobs.  Mr. Brown moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. Valdez.  Comments
followed on the upcoming Council work session to discuss the FY2011 budget.  Mr. Brown moved
to postpone for two weeks (to April 26, 2010), seconded by Mr Collins.  Mr. Hardgrave stated the
resolution, relative to the $1.4 milli on shortfall as previously identified by the City Treasurer for
next y ear’s budg et, is a key  component in balanc ing the  budg et. Comments followed on the
governing body’s fiscal responsibility, public perception on deferring the City’s annual payment to
the BOPU and indebtedness to future Council members, postponement to allow for information to
be received during the upcoming budget work session, and informal comments being made that are
causing fear to employ ees relative to fina ncial circumstances if the  City does make  the annua l
payment.  Motion to postpone carried. Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present
with the exception of Ms. Ash voting “no”.
[CA] “A Resolution authorizing submission of a Mineral Royalty Capital Construction Account
Grant (Chapter 3) application to the State Loan and Investment Board on behalf of the Governing
Body of the City  of Chey enne for the purpose of  Building  the East Pershing  Boule vard
Reconstruction Project.”  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried.  (#5200)
[CA] “A Resolution authorizing submission of a Mineral Royalty Capital Construction Account
Grant (Chapter 3) application to the State Loan and Investment Board on behalf of the Governing
Body of the City of Cheyenne for the purpose of Purchasing a Fire Engine for Station #3.”  Motion
