RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
April 26, 2010
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning
at 6 :00 p .m. i n C ouncil Chambers.  P resent w ere: M AYOR -  R ichard L.  K aysen; C OUNCIL
MEMBERS: Ms. Ash, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Collins, Mr. Pierson, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Spiker
and Mr. Valdez.  Absent:  Ms. Case.  Also present: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk; Barb Dorr, City
Treasurer, and Dan White, City Attorney.  The pledge of allegiance was recited.
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA]  are considered to be
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its
normal sequence on the agenda.)  Upon request by Mr. Brown, and pursuant to support received by
Mr. Collins and Ms. Ash, ag enda item #16 wa s removed f rom the Consent Ag enda. Mr. Spiker
moved to approve  remaining Consent Ag enda items, sec onded by Mr. Valde z. Motion carried.
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.
[CA] Minutes from Reg ular Meeting of the Gove rning Body on April 12, 2010.  Motion (per
Consent Agenda) carried.
[CA] Minutes from Vouchers Meeting of the Governing Body on April 19, 2010.  Motion (per
Consent Agenda) carried.
Mr. Brown moved to approve payment of vouchers as presented, seconded by Mr. Valdez.
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.
Mayor Kaysen opened a public hearing for the purpose of receiving comments and input for
the City of Cheyenne Comprehensive Consolidated Plan for 2010-2014 and the Annual Action Plan
for Fiscal Year 2010-2011 for the City of Cheyenne Community Development Block Grant (CDBG)
entitlement.  Deanne Widauf, Housing and Community Development Office, provided an overview
of the CDBG entitlement program and the Comprehensive Consolidated Plan, and cited some of the
identified Plan goals and objectives.  She noted the Consolidated Plan also incorporates the Annual
Action Pl an f or fi scal year 2 010-2011 fo r w hich th e City’s e stimated e ntitlement a llocation is
$580,253.00 with an anticipated additional $5,000 in program income.  Ms. Widauf described the
grant application process; public notifications and opportunities for input; oversig ht of the g rant
process by  the Adv isory Council, and the Advisory  Council’s review  and re commendations
involving elig ible applications.  She identi fied proposed re commended pr ojects and pr ogram
administration, and estimated funding allocation amount for each.  Upon inquiry regarding proposed
funding for a Parks and Recreation structural safety and restoration project, Rick Parish, Parks and
Recreation Direc tor, clar ified the proje ct involves re- siding a nd ener gy eff icient window
replacements for the Pioneer Park Activity Center.  Th ere being no further comments or input,
Mayor Kaysen advised the purpose of the hearing had been met and closed the hearing.
Mayor Kaysen welcomed students and their parents in the audience who were attending the
meeting in conjunction with a school project.
“An Ordinance annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, a parcel of land being situated
in Sections 12 and 13, Township 13 North, Range 67 West of the 6  Principal Meridian, Laramieth
County, Wyoming (located East of Southwest Drive, South of Interstate 80, West of BNSF Railroad,
and North of Woodenshoe Drive).”  Francis Shive; Doris Garcia; Ellie Sturman; Leo Garcia; Dave
Wilkinson; Kathleen Sheehan; Gay Woodhouse,  attorney representing residents along Southwest
Drive; D an F rank, F rank L aw O ffice, a ttorney re presenting Ms. Sheehan, a nd Jef f K etcham,
Laramie County Commissioner, spoke in opposition of the proposed annexation.  Among the topics
presented and discussed were:  City/County zoning regulations; health, safety and welfare concerns
involving the volume of train and vehicular traffic and affiliated traffic congestion and delays;
unknown property development plans by the developer; annexation criteria pursuant to Wyo. Stat.
§15-1-402; contiguity concerns relative to annexation of Swan Ranch area; county island creation;
mental a nd fi nancial st ress t o area residents a nd c oncerns in volving fu ture mon etary lia bility
involving infrastructure costs; request to eliminate Woodenshoe Drive  from the annexation area;
timeliness of emergency services response, and suggestion that a “sketch plan” be required to review
developer’s proposed  i nfrastructure, transportation and drainage solutions. Mike Basom, Dray,
Thomson and Dy ekman law  firm, attorne y repr esenting the devel oper, spoke in fa vor of the
annexation to enable the platting process to move forward to address issues raised involving safety
and traffic concerns.  He stated no changes to the area after annexation will be made until a plat is
prepared and approved by the g overning body.  Reporting  for Public Services Committee, Mr.
Collins moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Ms. Ash.  Discussion followed
and included: Area access; traffic and transportation issues; property owner’s (developer) request
for annexation; contiguity; area residents’ concerns that have been raised, and train traffic at the
crossing on Southwest Drive.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body
present with the exception of Mr. Valdez voting “no”. (#3889)
“An Ordinance amending Title 17, Zoning, Chapter 17.08, Zoning Districts Established and
Section 17.08.040 Cheyenne City map of the Cheyenne City Code, by changing the z oning
classification from PUD Planned Unit Development District to MR-2 Medium Density Residential-
Developing District f or L ots 1 through 14 inc lusive of Dry  Creek Twin Homes Subdivi sion,
Cheyenne, Wyoming, to be known as a portion of Woodhaven Subdivision (located west of College
Drive, between the Dry Creek Channel and McCann Avenue).”  Mr. Collins moved to approve on
second r eading, s econded b y M s. A sh.  M r. Sp iker d eclared a  c onflict o f in terest ( distant
relationship with the developer) and vacated Council Chambers.  Concern was expressed with uses
allowed under the MR-2 zone as approved by the Board of Adjustment.  Motion carried.  Voting
“yes” – all members of the governing body present in Council Chambers.  Following announcement
of the vote, Mr. Spiker returned to Council Chambers.
“An Ordinance amending Title 17, Zoning, Chapter 17.08, Zoning Districts Established and
Section 17.08. 040 Chey enne City  map of the Chey enne City  Code, by  chang ing the  zoning
classification from MR-1 Medium Density Residential-Established District to MUB Mixed Use with
Business Emphasis District for Lots 1 and 2; and the north 66-feet of Lots 3 and 4, Block 195,
Original City, Cheyenne, Wyoming (located at the southwest corner of the intersection of E. 23rd
Street and Russell Avenue).” Dr. Stephanie Erlich, homeowner in the area, spoke in favor of the
zone chang e and pr oposed use of the building w ithin the hist oric ne ighborhood.  Rev. Dan
McKinley, SonRise Church of the Nazarene (property owner), requested approval of the ordinance,
advising the object of the re-zoning request involves potential sale of the property to assist with
building a new church; that the church has a contract with a funeral home interested in the property,
and that this type of use would be suitable for the location.  Mr. Collins moved to approve on second
reading with Planning Commission restriction that “the use be limited to allow only a funeral home
and related services,” seconded by Mr. Spiker.  Comments were made regarding restricted property
use if sale of the property to the funeral home did not occur, and possible further discussion on the
topic at the next Publ ic Services Committee meeting.  Upon inquiry on whether other re-zoning
actions had involved r estrictions on the ty pe of businesse s allowed, L isa Paffor d,
Building/Development Office, provided information on two recent conditional zoning restrictions
approved by the governing body.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the g overning
body present.
“A Resolution authorizing the transfer of funds in the approximate amount of $400,000.00
from the City’s Solid Waste Management Fund to the General Fund.” (Postponed on 04-12-10).
Reporting for F inance Committee, Mr. B rown moved to a dopt, seconded by Mr. Pierson.  Mr.
Brown moved to postpone for two weeks (to May 10, 2010), seconded by Dr. Rinne.  It was noted
that the Committee’s recommendation to postpone was to allow time for information to be received
at the Council’s budget work session (held April 22 ).  Motion to postpone failed.  Voting “no” –nd
all members of the governing body present.  Comments followed regarding the probability that the
funds would n ot be needed relative to FY 2010 employee furlough days or to balance the current
fiscal year budget, but that the funds will be needed to help balance the FY2011 budget; time frame
on transferring the amount into the g eneral fund, and replenishing the Solid W aste Management
Fund.  Main motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present with the
exception of Ms. Ash, Mr. Collins and Dr. Rinne voting “no”. (#5204)
“A Resolution amending Resolution No. 2852 establishing a payment schedule for Stage II
of the Cheyenne Water Project.” (Postponed on 04-12-10).  Mr. Brown moved to adopt, seconded
by Ms. Broyles.  Mr. Brown moved to postpone for two weeks (to May 10, 2010).  Motion failed
due to lack of a second to the motion.  Di scussion followed and included: “on/off budgeting”
practices; current economic conditions; governing body’s fiscal responsibility; reduced municipal
funding from the state ; public perc eption on defe rring the City ’s annua l pay ment to BOPU;
indebtedness to future governing bodies; suggestion of inserting a specific date in the resolution as
to when payment(s) to repay BOPU would be made; ba lancing the budget being a separate issue
from 5  Penny Sales Tax; reducing budget costs through attrition instead of further reductions inth
employee work force; re visiting the re solution topic next fiscal y ear relative to status of budg et
revenues/expenditures; partial payments scenario; employee job security; legal reviews reflecting
the annual payment is not a requirement, and concern with the effect on public services if additional
reductions in work force occur.  Upon inquiry regarding how many employee jobs may be affected
if the $1,000,000 payment is not deferred, City Treasurer Barb Dorr responded that approximately
15 jobs equates to $1,000,000.  Discussion continued and included:  Options for consideration (i.e.
use general fund reserves for the payment; impose additional reductions in work force, or delay the
payment); willingness by BOPU to m ake the payment; public perception; reluctance to i mpose
further work force reductions; across-the-board employee salary cuts and job sharing as cost savings
measures, and status  of budget savings suggestions submitted from e mployees.  Ma yor Kaysen
provided the f ollowing information in response to discussion topics: Post poning action will not
achieve a balanced budget required to be approved by July 1 ; stronger revenues than anticipatedst
for next fiscal year could allow for  a payment(s) to BOPU to be ac ted upon; resolution wording
providing for  suspension of the bond pay ment, not forg iveness of ma king the  pay ment; non-
opposition by the BOPU regarding delay of the City’s payment; clarification that bondholders will
be paid, and that water rates, per information provided by BOPU, will not increase based upon the
City’s suspension of pay ment.  He further informed that employ ee suggestions that have be en
submitted are being pursued and some have merit; general fund reserves amount is “borderline”
pursuant to the Resolution on the level of cash reserves, and that across-the-board employee salary
reductions had been a nalyzed.  M r. Spiker moved to amend r esolution wording  in the Now,
Therefore, Be It Resolved paragraph as follows: “ ... that Resolution No. 2852, adopted F ebruary
11, 1985, is hereby amended to temporarily suspend the $1,000,000.00 pay ment to the Boa rd of
Public Utilities which is due on December 1, 2010, FOR A PERIOD OF 1 YEAR.”, and strike the
remainder of  th e s entence, s econded b y M r. B rown.  [C lerk’s n ote: R emainder o f th e c urrent
sentence to be removed reads: “and the suspension of said payment will remain in force and effect
until such tim e as the  governing body elects to repea l the suspension.”] Ms. Dorr advised the
proposed amendment would mean there would then be a $2,000,000 burden on the general fund
budget for that year, and commented generally on budget considerations.  Comment followed on the
intent of the motion to amend, which was not to incur a $2 million dollar payment within a fiscal
year, but to insert a date specific for the City to make payment.  Mr. Brown withdrew his second to
the motion. Upon request for amendment wording assistance, City Attorney Dan White suggested
proposed language could be as follows: “ ... that Resolution No. 2852, adopted February 11, 1985,
is h ereby a mended to  te mporarily su spend the  $1 ,000,000.00 p ayment t o th e Board of  Pub lic
Utilities which is due on December 1, 2010 until December 1, 2011, and to temporarily suspend the
$1,000,000.00 payment to the Board of Public Utilities which is due on December 1, 2011 until
December 1, 2012.”  Mr. Spiker moved to amend per Mr. White’s suggested wording, seconded by
Mr. Brown.  Comments followed on specifying payment dates vs. current wording addressing one
payment and BOPU approval with the current wording.  Mr. Spiker withdrew his motion to amend
with concurrence by Mr. Brown as second to the motion.  Mr. Spiker moved to amend the paragraph
as follows: “ ... temporarily suspend the $1,000,000.00 payment to the Board of Public Utilities
which is due on December 1, 2010 UNTIL DECEMBER 1, 2012.", and revise affiliated language
as necessary in the resolution, seconded by Mr. Brown.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” –
all members of the governing body present.  Upon inquiry, Ms. Dorr advised yearly payroll costs
in total is approximately $34,000,000.  Comments followed on reducing employees’ salaries to make
up the $1,000,000 def icit.  Ms. Dorr a dvised a g ood portion of the $34,000,000 pay roll budget
involves health insurance coverage costs and a  percentage cut in pay would not affe ct insurance
costs unless employees were asked to pay a larger portion of the insurance payment.  Main motion
as ame nded ca rried. V oting “ yes” – Mr . Br own, Ms. Br oyles, Mr. Pierson , Dr. Rinne, Mayor
Kaysen.  Voting “no” - Ms. Ash, Mr. Collins, Dr. Rinne and Mr. Spiker. (#5205)
“A Resolution authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to s ign a Final Plat for BOPU
Subdivision, a replat of Block 149, Original City, Cheyenne, Wyoming (located at the northwest
corner of the intersection of Snyder Avenue and W. 24  Street).”  Brad Emmons, AVI, agent forth
BOPU, advised the plat is for a new BOPU administration building, noting the preliminary plat had
been previously approved as well as a resolution relative to State Land and Investment Board (SLIB)

and area enhancement for the Snyder/24th site, and past Council work session discussion relative

voting “no”.  (#5206)
[CA] “A Resolution acknowledging receipt of and approving the “West Allison Road Corridor

“A Resolution certifying that the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne is in agreement

Brown moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. Valdez.  Mr. Brown moved to am end b y s ubstitute,

Commissioners have the item on their upcoming meeting agenda.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting
“yes” – a ll members of the g overning body  prese nt.  Comm ent was ma de supp orting other
communities’ needs involvin g the consensus block grant process.  Main motion as amende d by
substitute carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.  (#5208)

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
May 10, 2010
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning
at 6 :00 p .m. i n C ouncil Chambers.  P resent w ere: M AYOR -  R ichard L.  K aysen; C OUNCIL
MEMBERS: Ms. Ash, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Ms. Case, Mr. Collins, Mr. Pierson, Dr. Rinne, Mr.
Spiker and Mr. Valdez.  Also present: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk; Barb Dorr, City Treasurer, and
Dan White, City Attorney.  The pledge of allegiance was recited.
RECOGNITION O F F IRE A ND R ESCUE C HIEF G UY E . C AMERON O N H IS
RETIREMENT.  City  Clerk Carol I ntlekofer rea d a May oral proc lamation into the rec ord
recognizing Chief Cameron for his years of service to the residents of Cheyenne and proclaiming
May 19, 2010 Guy  E. Cameron D ay.  May or Ka ysen pre sented Chief Came ron with the
proclamation, a plaque of appreciation for his 32 years of dedicated service to the City and a 100
year anniversary Fire Department commemorative medallion.  Appreciation was expressed by
Mayor Kaysen and members of the governing body on the Chief’s a chievements benefitting the
department and community.  Chief Cameron commented on his positive employment experience
with the City, and expressed appreciation to past and present mayors and members of the governing
body for their trust and confidence in him and also to co-staff with whom he has worked over the
years.
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA]  are considered to be
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its
normal sequence on the agenda.)  Mr. Pierson moved to approve Consent Agenda items, seconded
by Mr. Spiker.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.
[CA] Minutes from Reg ular Meeting of the Gove rning Body on April 26, 2010.  Motion (per
Consent Agenda) carried.
[CA] Minutes from Vouchers Meeting of the Gover ning Body on May  3, 2010.  Motion (per
Consent Agenda) carried.
Mr. Valdez moved to approve payment of vouchers as presented, seconded by Ms. Broyles.
Mr. Brown declared a conflict of interest (business interest) due to a voucher payable to Ameri-Tech
HVAC Services, and vacated Council Chambers. Upon inquiry regarding a voucher payable to
Union Pacific Railroad ($5,561.57 for flagging costs), Doug Vetter, City Engineer, explained the
majority of flagging expenses should be completed relative to the Norris Viaduct project.  Motion
carried.  Vo ting “ yes” – a ll m embers of the governing bo dy pr esent i n Co uncil Ch ambers.
Following announcement of the vote, Mr. Brown returned to Council Chambers.
Mayor Kaysen opened a public hearing for the purpose of receiving comments and input to
determine whether compliance of annexation conditions required by Wyo. Stat § 15-1-402 exists
for a parcel of land being situated in a portion of Tracts 7 and 10, Dell Range Addition 2  Filing;nd
Lot 3, Smithson View, together with a portion of Carla  Drive, Thomas Road and College Drive,
Laramie County, Wyoming (generally located north of and adjacent to Carla Drive, between Ridge
Road and College Drive).  Don Threewitt, Urban Planning, reviewed requirements and compliance
of c onditions c ontained in  Wy o. St at. § 15-1-402, in cluding s pecific in formation in dicating
compliance with Wy o. Stat. §15-1-402(a )(i) throug h (vi). Robin Huber que stioned whether  a
development proposal had been submitted, and requested the name of the developer.  Mr. Threewitt
clarified a development plan had not yet been submitted, and provided the names of the developers,
who are the property owners.  Mayor Kaysen advised any questions involving the prope rty to be
annexed could be communicated to Mr. Threewitt.  There being no further comments or input and
purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor Kaysen closed the hearing.
“An Ordinance amending Title 17, Zoning, Chapter 17.08, Zoning Districts Established and
Section 17.08.040 Chey enne City  map of the Chey enne City  Code, by  chang ing the  zoning
classification from PUD Planned Unit Development District to MR-2 Medium Density Residential-
Developing District f or L ots 1 through 14 inc lusive of Dry  Creek T win Homes Subdivision,
Cheyenne, Wyoming, to be known as a portion of Woodhaven Subdivision (located west of College
Drive, between the Dry Creek Channel and McCann Avenue).”   Due to any perception of conflict
involving distant family  ties with the develo per, Mr. Spiker dec lared a conf lict of interest a nd
vacated Council Chambers.  Reporting for Public Services Committee, Ms. Ash moved to approve
on third and final r eading, seconded by Mr. Collins.  M s. Ash moved to amend by  subst itute,
seconded by Mr. Collins.  It was noted, pursuant to discussion and amendments recommended by
the Public Services Committee, the substitute includes the addition of the word “conditional” in the
ordinance entitlement and in Section 1.; inserts a new section which reads as follows: “That the zone
change was conditioned by  the Gove rning Body to allow only those uses by  right and uses by
administrative approval.  No other uses shall be allowed by Board Approval.  All other requirements
for the MR-2 Medium Density Residential-Developing District will remain as outlined in Section
17.48 of the Cheyenne City Code.”, and includes the following staff recommendation: “A condition
be placed on the approval of the zone change that the applicant record a “Notice of Conditional
Zone” to provide public notification of specific restriction to prospective buyers”.  It was further
noted the intent of the changes is to protect the neighborhood by providing that any development
of apartment buildings/complexes would require the consent of the governing body.  M otion to
amend carried. Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present in Council Chambers.
Main motion as amended by substitute carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body
present in Council Chambers.  Following announcement of the vote, Mr. Spiker returned to Council
Chambers. (#3890)
“An Ordinance amending Title 17, Zoning, Chapter 17.08, Zoning Districts Established and
Section 1 7.08.040 Chey enne City  map of the Chey enne City  Code, by  chang ing the  zoning
classification from MR-1 Medium Density Residential-Established District to MUB Mixed Use with
Business Emphasis District for Lots 1 and 2; and the north 66- feet of Lots 3 and 4, Bloc k 195,
Original City, Cheyenne, Wyoming (located at the southwest corner of the intersection of E. 23rd
Street and Russell Avenue).”  Ms. Ash moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by
Mr. Collins.  Ms. Ash moved to amend by  substitute, seconded by Mr. Collins.  I t was noted,
pursuant to discussion and ame ndments rec ommended by the Public Services Committee, the
substitute includes the addition of the word “conditional” in the ordinance entitlement and in Section
1.; inserts a new section which reads as follows: “That the zone change was conditioned by  the
Governing Body as follows : The uses by  right shall be limited to # 1. Funeral home and re lated
services within the existing building or #2. Church and related services within the existing building,
no other uses shall be allowed by Administrative or Board Approval.  All other requirements for the
MUB Mixed Use with Business Emphasis District will remain as outlined in Chapter 17.88 of the
Cheyenne City Code.”, and includes following staff recommendation: “A condition be placed on the
approval of the zone change that the applicant record a “Notice of Conditional Zone” to provide
public no tification of s pecific re striction to  pr ospective bu yers.”  Upon inquiry, L isa Paffor d,
Assistant Development Director, clarified related (funeral) services would include offices related
to the funeral business, and services provided for an actual funeral and viewing areas.  Sh e also
advised a crematorium use would not be allowed in this type of zoning district, and to change the
use to include other services would require re-zoning.  Comments followed on the historical nature
of the neighborhood; support received from neighbors concerning the proposed use of the property,
limiting use of the site, and request for postponement received from the applicant’s agent.  Motion
to amend carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.  Ms. Ash moved to postpone
for two weeks (to May 24, 2010), seconded by Mr. Collins.  John Steil, Steil Surveying Services,
agent for applicant, advised the proposed purchaser of the property has inquired about adding a
garage onto the existing building to house funeral service vehicles and with the current language this
would not be allowed.  He noted support for postponement, suggesting the governing body could
consider amending the wording a t the curr ent meeting and move f orward with approva l of the
ordinance.  Motion to postpone carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.
“An Ordinance appropriating moneys for the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming for the various
purposes in conducting the municipal government of said city and fixing the amount of general and
special taxes as part of the revenue required to meet the said a ppropriation, all for the fiscal year
beginning July 1, 2010, and ending June 30, 2011” -- referred to Finance Committee (sponsor - Ms.
Broyles).
“An Ordinance approving and esta blishing revised water rates, as recommended by the
Board of Public Utilities to be effective January 1, 2011” –  referred to Finance Committee (sponsor
- Mr. Brown).
“An Ordinance approving and establishing revised sewer rates, as recommended by the
Board of Public Utilities to be effective January 1, 2011” –  referred to Finance Committee (sponsor
- Mr. Brown).
“An Ordinance approving and establishing revised tap fees, system development fees, pump
station fees a nd other re lated fees recommended by the B oard of Public Utilities to be e ffective
January 1, 2011” -- referred to Finance Committee (sponsor - Mr. Brown).
“An Ordinance approving and establishing revised administrative fees, service charges and
penalties as recommended by the Board of Public Utili ties to be eff ective January 1, 2011” --
referred to Finance Committee (sponsor - Mr. Brown).
“An Ordinance approving and establishing revised septic waste dump rates and  commercial
waste rates recommended by the B oard of Public Utili ties to be eff ective January 1, 2011” --
referred to Finance Committee (sponsor - Mr. Brown).
[CA] “A Resolution adopting the Cit y of Cheyenne Comprehensive Consolidated Plan 2010-
2014, which has been prepared by the Cheyenne Housing and Community Development Office”.
Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#5209)
[CA] “A Resolution supporting providing funds from the City’s Optional One Percent Sales Tax
Infrastructure Development Fund for the purpose of  extending infrastructure required to provide
non-stop jet service between Cheyenne, Wyoming, and Dallas, Texas, on American Airlines”  (2007
- 2010 1% Sales Tax).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (not to exceed $200,000.00).  (#5210)
[CA] “A Resolution authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a Final Plat for Crow Creek
Water Reclamation Subdivision, situated in a portion of the SE¼ of Section 3, T.13N., R.66W., 6th
P.M., Cheyenne, Wyoming (l ocated south of S. I ndustrial Road, ea st of S. Col lege Drive)” --

[CA] Preliminary Plat for The Bluffs Subdivision, 8th Filing, situated in a portion of the SW¼

[CA] County Preliminary Plat for Deerhaven Subdivision, a replat of Tract 6 and a portion of

[CA] Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and the Boys and Girls Club of Cheyenne, Inc.,
to lease the building located at 1700 Snyder Avenue, Cheyenne, WY.  Motion (per Consent Agenda)

[CA] Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and the Cheyenne Symphony Orchestra for use

[CA] Amendment #1 to Contract #5327, Transmission Easement License Agreement, between the

[CA] Consideration of B id #M- 15-10 for a contract be tween the  City of Chey enne a nd J TL

MEETING: City Council | DATE: 2010-05-24 | CLIP_ID: 155 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 155

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
May 24, 2010
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning
at 6 :00 p .m. i n C ouncil Chambers.  P resent w ere: M AYOR -  R ichard L.  K aysen; C OUNCIL
MEMBERS: Ms. Ash, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Ms. Case, Mr. Collins, Mr. Pierson, Mr. Spiker and
Mr. V aldez.  A bsent: Dr . Ri nne.  A lso pr esent: Ca rol I ntlekofer, City  Cle rk; B arb Do rr, Ci ty
Treasurer, and Erik Rasmussen, Deputy City Attorney.  The pledge of allegiance was recited.
George Parks, Director of the Wyoming Association of Municipalities (WAM), recognized
Mrs. Johnna Lissman & the 9  Grade Class at Carey Jr. High School and the City of Cheyenne asth
a recipient of WAM’s Community Hero Award for a special community enhancement project.
Jason Sanchez, Director of Grounds and Facilities Maintenance, Parks & Recreation Dept., advised
Carey Jr. High  School’s 9th grade class has assisted the City with a community clean-up day for
the past 13 y ears providing swee ping, c leaning and debr is pickup. Ms. L issman expressed
appreciation to the City, WAM, Carey Jr. High School administration and students, and City staff
involved for providing an opportunity to s tudents to c ombine government education with civic
projects.  Ma yor Ka ysen r ecognized and  tha nked M s. L issman a nd Ca rey Jr. H igh f or the ir
involvement in the community.
Expression of Appreciation to Mayor’s Youth Council and State of the Youth Report.  Mayor
Kaysen and Jan Spires, Mayor’s Youth Council coordinator, commended members of the 2009-2010
Mayor’s Youth Council and presented each with a certificate and gift of appreciation. Youth Council
members recognized were: Kelsey James, Alissa Alyward, Kadi Davis, Samantha Heitsch, Josh
Herr, Karolyn Hopfensperger, Dean Jackson, Ashley Ortiz, Taylar Perez, Katie Poffenbarger, Gary
Sayler, and Mishayla Still.  Kelsey James, Youth Council President, provided a State of the Youth
report and described various accomplishments and issues they supported during the past school year,
and commented on members’ positive interactions and learning experiences through involvement
with local government issues.  Mayor Kaysen expressed appreciation to the members for their time,
commitment and dedication as Youth Council members.  
Recognition of Public Works Project of the Year 2010 Award from the American Public
Works Association, Rocky Mountain Chapter, to the City of Cheyenne for the Dry Creek Sheridan
Reach Flood Control Improvement Project. Doug Vetter, City Engineer, advised it was an honor to
receive this award for a very worthy project that addresses very high life safety and property damage
concerns along Dry Creek.  Mr . Vetter recognized the engineering firm of Ayres Associates and
general contractor, Tarco, that assisted with the project and presented Mayor Kaysen with the plaque
that had been received.
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA]  are considered to be
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its
normal sequence on the agenda.) Upon request by Ms. Broyles, and pursuant to support received by
Mr. Collins and Mr. Brow n, agenda item #30(a) was removed f rom the Consent Ag enda. Upon
request by Mr. Collins, and pursuant to support rec eived by Mr. Pierson and Mr. B rown, agenda
item #29(b) wa s removed f rom the Consent Ag enda.  Mr . Collins moved to approve  remaining
Consent Agenda items, seconded by Mr. Brown. Motion carried. Voting “yes” – all members of the
governing body present.
[CA] Minutes from Reg ular Meeting of the Governing Body on May  10, 2010.  Motion (per
Consent Agenda) carried.
[CA] Minutes from Vouchers Meeting of the Gove rning Body on May 17, 2010.  Motion (per
Consent Agenda) carried.
Minutes from Special Meetings of the Governing Body on May 4, 2010 and May 18, 2010.
Mayor Kaysen and Ms. Case advised they would not be participating in the discussion or vote as
they were not participants of the governing body for which the Special Meetings were held.  Mr.
Pierson moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Valdez. Motion carried.  Voting “yes” –  all members
of the governing body present with the exception of Ms. Case and Mayor Kaysen who did not vote.
Mr. Brown moved to approve payment of vouchers as presented, seconded by Mr. Spiker.
Upon inquiry regarding a vouche r payable to Expr ess Towing and Rec overy ($1,755.00), Doug
Vetter, City Engineer, provided information on the service s provided, which involved towing  of
vehicles in conjunction with a crack seal street project.  Upon inquiry regarding vouchers payable
to several bands for upcoming performances at the Depot Plaza for the Hispanic Festival, City

reviewed vouc hers pa yable to Ne eds, I nc. ($2,000) , C DBG grant funds f or food to qualifie d

membership in WAM is effective for the remainder of this fiscal y ear.  Motion carr ied.  Voting
“yes” – all members of the governing body present with the exception of Mr. Collins who did not
vote.
Mayor Kaysen opened a public hearing for the purpose of receiving public comments on the
proposed vacation of dedicated public right-of-way of Civitan Place between Cribbon Ave. and Dey
Ave., the dedicated alley located in Civitan Park between West Leisher Road and Civitan Place, and

map of the proposed property to be vacated and explained the purpose of vacating the property is
to allow for a new school to be built within the park area.  He stated a land trade between the City
and school district would also be involved.  D ennis Auker, LCSD#1, clarified the property to the
City being considered for exchange is 5 acres in Sun Valley park located on 12  St. He advised anth
appraisal had been done for both properties t o be traded and adjustments have bee n made for an
equal trade.  He advised there is a proposed Greenway along Cribbon Ave.  There being no further
comments and purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor Kaysen closed the hearing.
“An Ordinance amending Title 17, Zoning, Chapter 17.08, Zoning Districts Established and

Original City, Cheyenne, Wyoming (located at the southwest corner of the intersection of E. 23rd
Street and Russell Avenue).” Reporting for Public Services Committee, Ms. Ash moved to approve
on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Collins.  Ms. Ash moved to amend by substitute which
inserts a new section #4 to read as follows: “Placement of up to a 750 square foot addition and/or
accessory building would be permitted” and by renumbering remaining sections, seconded by Mr.
Collins.  Discussion followed on proposed use for the building as a funeral home and the need for
an addition to the building for a garage to store hearses.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” –
all members of the governing body present with the exception of Ms. Case and Mr. Valdez voting
“no”.  Main motion as amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.
(#3891)

purposes in conducting the municipal government of said city and fixing the amount of general and

“An Ordinance approving and esta blishing revised water rates, as recommended by the
Board of Public Utilities to be effective January 1, 2011.” Mr. Brown moved to approve on second
reading, seconded by Ms. Broyles.  Mr. Brown moved to amend by substitute to amend the rate
increase to 3.4% , seconded by Ms. Broyles. Upon inquiry, Tim Wilson, BOPU Director, clarified
the BOPU approved a 6% r ate increase based on the All Urba n Consumer Price I ndex which
encompasses all urban c onsumers water and sewage maintenance and tak es into account r ates
nationwide. He provided information the Board considered relative to determining the amount of
increase, which includes re view of c ost areas during budget preparations, specific infrastructure
projects and p ersonnel costs.   Concer n was e xpressed regarding the basis for the  proposed 6%
increase, and the type of Consumer Price Index information utilized relative to the original intent
of providing for annual review and/or revisions to rates.  Discussion included index information (not
specific to the state or region) used in ascertaining the percentage of rate increase; infrastructure and
BOPU budget needs; increased cost of employee health and pension funding; amount of BOPU
water and sewer reserves; projected revenue to be received if a 3.4% increase is implemented, and
concern with maintaining infrastructure, BOPU loans for current and future specific projects (re:
Southern Transmission project) and consumer rate increase effect.  Motion to amend carried. Voting
“yes” – all members of the governing body present with the exception of Ms. Case voting “ no”.

County Preliminary Plat for Deerhaven Subdivision, a replat of Tract 6 and a portion of
Tract 7, Allison Tracts; Tract 2, Hermann Meadows Subdivision; Lot 2 and a portion of Lot 1,

Jefferson Roads, east of S. Greeley Highway).  Kelly Hefner, Benchmark Engineers and agent for

member of South Side Water and Sewer District, stated it was her understanding that individual
property owners could not annex, and that any annexation had to involve the entire District.  She
advised many of the c urrent property  owner s have no ide a about the a nnexation
agreement/requirement, and spoke in support of the motion to postpone.  It was advised further
concerns regarding recommendations were raised at the Public Services Committee and should
also be addressed during t he postponement.  Mo tion to postpone carried.  Vo ting “yes” – a ll
members of the governing body present in Council Chambers.  Following announcement of the
vote, Mr. Spiker returned to Council Chambers.

Consideration of B id #M-15-10 for  a contr act between the City of Chey enne and JTL
Group, Inc., d/b/a Knife River of Cheyenne, WY, for the FY 2010 City Overlay project.  Mr.
Brown moved to ac cept the bid in an a mount not to ex ceed $1,062,173.00, s econded by Mr.
Pierson. Upon inquiry regarding the Vandehei portion of the project, Doug Vetter, City Engineer,
discussed the following: Drainage issues on Vandehei, west of Yellowstone; necessity for overlay
work; schedule for WYDOT’s new Vandehei interchange; proposal for new center medians and
removing calming islands (a/k/a “ side-ins”); geometric design; traffic, safety and maintena nce
issues involving the calming islands; opportunity to improve street design features; concern with
setting back the project time frame, and modifying the project without re-bidding.  Comments
followed both for and against the calming islands, and slowing of traffic.  Joe Wiesen, Knife River
representative, clarified they would be willing to work with leaving the calming islands in place
and could work around them to get the project done. Upon inquiry regarding other locations of
calming islands in Cheyenne, Mr. Vetter advised the only other location they have calming islands
is on 12  Street and c larified there has to be a de monstrated need bef ore these are installed.th
Discussion follo wed on snow plow issues with navig ating around the  islands, infrastruc ture,
calming islands and pros and cons, potential alterations to the project, and long term cost effect
regarding maintenance.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Vetter advised they will not get the best overlay trying
to work around the side-ins and paving around them will be a problem. Upon inquiry, Jim Elias,
Public Works Director, advised it is a cha llenge working around the islands with plows, street
sweepers and Sanitation trucks, indicating they can not plow between the islands and curbs. Upon
inquiry, E rik Rasmussen, D eputy City  At torney, a dvised if  the  bid  do cuments ha d s pecific
specifications intermingle d for a ll of the p rojects (3), t he integ rity of the bid could be
compromised if it were modified for one of the projects. Concerns were expressed with changing
the Vandehei project as bid, and discussion was held on utilization of calming islands; change
orders, value engineering and contract modifications; fairness to bidders; intermingling of all
projects in bids received, pedestrian and traffic safety issues. Mr. Collins moved to postpone to
June 14, 2010 to allow for  leg al revie w of the issues r aised during  discussion and the bid,
seconded by Ms. Broy les.  Motion to postpone carried.  V oting “yes” – a ll members of the
governing body present with the exception of Ms. Case and Mayor Kaysen voting “no”.
[CA] Consideration of Bid #M-22-10 for a contract between the City of Cheyenne and JTL
Group, Inc., d/b/a Knife River of Cheyenne, WY, for the 2009 Cheyenne Transit Program Bus
Stop Upgrades project -- referred to Finance Committee. 
[CA] Designation of Official Voting Delegate and Alternate Delegate for the City of Cheyenne
at the 2010 Wyoming Association of Municipalities convention.  Motion (per Consent Agenda)
carried (Mayor Kaysen Official Voting Delegate; Councilman Spiker Alternate Delegate).
Announcement was made of a public hearing to be held Monday, June 14, 2010 at 6:00
p.m. in City Council Chambers for proposed budgets for the City of Cheyenne and Board of Public
Utilities for fiscal year 2011.
Under other business, Ms. Broyles announced that a Calcutta will be held Thursday, June
3, 2010  beginning at 7:00 p.m. at Uncle Charlie’s Lounge, 6001 Yellowstone Road, Cheyenne,
WY, in conjunction with the annual Uncle Charlie’s DONE golf tournament to be held June 5,
2010 at the Airport Golf Club (15% of proceeds to benefit the Cheyenne Boys and Girls Club).
Comments followed regarding standing water observed at the Romero  Park parking lot
after the last snow storm.   Todd Feezer, Assistant Director, Parks and Recreation Dept., clarified
mitigation work in the area has been done to reduce or eliminate any standing water in the future.
In response to a c omment regarding water flooding the par k located ne ar Sunrise School, Mr.
Feezer advised irrigation systems in the parks are on a computer controlled system and he was not
aware of any water problems but would check into the matter.
There being no further  business to come bef ore the g overning body, the meeting  was
officially adjourned at 8:50 p.m. 
 
 Submitted by,
 
 
 
 Carol Intlekofer, MMC
 City Clerk

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
June 14, 2010
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning
at 6 :00 p .m. i n C ouncil Chambers.  P resent w ere: M AYOR -  R ichard L.  K aysen; C OUNCIL
MEMBERS - Ms. Ash, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Ms. Case, Mr. Pierson, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Spiker and
Mr. Valdez.  A bsent: Mr. Collins.  A lso present: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk; Barb Dorr, City
Treasurer, and Dan White, City Attorney.  The pledge of allegiance was recited.
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA]  are considered to be
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its
normal se quence on  the  a genda.)  Ma yor Kaysen a nnounced th at A genda Item #1 9 c ontains a
clerical error reflecting the item had been referred to Public Services Committee; it should instead
be listed as the Finance Committee.  Upon request by Ms. Case, and pursuant to support received
by Mr. S piker and Ms. Broyles, agenda item #19 wa s removed. Ms. Case  requested removal of
Agenda Item #32(c) with Mayor Kaysen advising the item is a referral to Committee and was not
eligible to b e removed from the Consent Agenda.  U pon request by Ms. Case, and pursuant to
support received by Mr. Spiker and Mr Pierson, agenda item #21 was removed from the Consent
Agenda.  Mr. Brown moved to approve remaining Consent Agenda items, seconded by Mr. Spiker.
Motion carried. Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.
[CA] Minutes from Reg ular Meeting of the Gover ning Body on May 24, 2010.  Motion (per
Consent Agenda) carried.
[CA] Minutes from Vouchers Meeting of the Gove rning Body on J une 7, 2010.  Motion (per
Consent Agenda) carried.
Mr. Brown and Mr. Valdez each declared a conflict of interest (business interest) due to
vouchers payable to Ameri-Tech HVAC Services and radio station KYOY respectively, and vacated
Council Chambers. Upon inquiry , City  Trea surer Barb Dor r and City  Eng ineer Doug  Vetter
provided information available concerning a voucher payable to M&F Towing and Recovery LLC,
towing of vehicles i n conjunction with the street overlay project.  Mr. Spiker moved to approve
payment of vouchers as presented, seconded by Mr. Pierson. Upon inquiry, Dennis Pino, Solid
Waste Direc tor, provided infor mation conce rning a vouche r payable to Pioneer  Printing for
residential recycling program informational brochures, and Vicki Nemecek, Assistant Public Works
Director, provided information affiliated with a voucher payable to the Wyoming Tribune Eagle for
5  Penny Sales Tax brochures.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing bodyth
present in Council C hambers. Following announcement of the vote, Mr . Brown and Mr. Valdez
returned to Council Chambers.
Mayor Kaysen opened a public hearing for the purpose of receiving public comments on the
proposed budget for the City of Cheyenne and Board of Public Utilities (BOPU) for fiscal year 2011.
City Treasurer Barb Dorr provided a brief summary on the proposed budget explaining the total
appropriation is $119,528,474.00 of which $46,260,700.00 comprises the general fund with a total
City budget of $66,648,774. 00.  The proposed BOPU budget amount is $52,879,700.00.  S he
reported department heads met the goal of a 12% budget cut from the 2010 budget, and provided
other budget information relative to a suspended $1,000,000.00 BOPU payment and savings from
the employee volunteer early retirement offering.  Randy Hayes, BOPU Administration Manager,
provided a brief summary on the proposed BOPU budget explaining the water fund appropriation
is $43,163,700.00 and the sewer fund budget is $9,716,000.00.  He provided further information on
anticipated revenue amounts for each fund, potential grant assistance, and amounts to be funded out
of cash.  There  being no fur ther comments and purpose  of the he aring having been me t, Mayor
Kaysen closed the hearing.
“An Ordinance appropriating moneys for the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming for the various
purposes in conducting the municipal government of said city and fixing the amount of general and
special taxes as part of the revenue required to meet the said appropriation, all for the fiscal year
beginning July 1, 2010, and ending  J une 30, 2011.”  E d Melko questioned whe ther propose d
sanitation fee increases were included in the ordinance with Barb Dorr, City Treasurer, responding
that the FY2011 budget includes a 1% increase and if there were any increases following budget
approval, a budget reappropriation could correct the amounts.  Reporting for Finance Committee,
Mr. Brown moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Pierson. Dr. Rinne moved
to amend the ordinance to add funding for a child care assistant position for Parks and Recreation’s
Latch Key program in the amount of $41,877.72 to include benefits, and, in the affiliated revenue
line item, to add $42,000.00 to reflect funding to be received to cover the position expense with the
condition that if program revenue generated can not cover position expenses, funding will come out
of the Parks and Recreation budget, seconded by Mr. Spiker.  Rick Parish, Parks and Recreation
Director, e xplained th ere a re tw o f acilities th at have  the  L atch K ey a nd Sum mer R ec. Ca mp
programs (Pioneer Park a nd David R. Rom ero Youth Activity Center) and stated his belief  that
enough revenue can be g enerated through registration fees t o cover the position ex pense.  H e
reviewed registration participation numbers, waiting lists for those interested in registering for the
programs, and program benefits to community youth.  Up on inquiry, Ms. Do rr and Mr. Parish
reviewed program registration fees. Discussion included program benefits and revenue generation,
and Parks and Recreation loan repayment schedule affiliated with construction of the Romero Youth
Activity Center.  Motion to amend c arried.  Voting  “yes” – a ll members of the g overning body
present with the exception of Ms. Case voting “no”.  Upon inquiry regarding the possibility of filling
the position by former employees affected by the reduction in work force, Mr. Parish advised job
description criteria must be met with Mayor Kaysen requesting Mr. Parish communicate with the
Human Resources Director on the topic.  Comments followed on funding included in the budget for
the City’s annual membership dues to the Wy oming Association of Municipali ties (WAM) and
membership services provided.  Ms. Ash moved to strike budget funding for the dues in the amount
of $40,485.00, sec onded by  Dr. Rinne.  Discussion followed a nd included support of WAM
membership, City’s representation on the WAM Board, and WAM’s budgetary assistance to its
membership by reducing next fiscal year’s dues amount by 10%.  Motion failed.  Voting “yes” – Ms.
Ash and Dr. Rinne.  Voting “no” – Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Ms. Case, Mr. Pierson, Mr. Spiker, Mr.
Valdez and Mayor Kaysen.  Main motion as amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the
governing body present with the exception of Ms. Ash voting “no”. (#3892)
“An Ordinance app roving and establishing revised water rates, as re commended by the
Board of Public Utilities to be effective January 1, 2011.”  Eric Russell questioned the relationship
of proposed fee increases for this ordinance (and following affiliated ordinances on the agenda) with
the City’s deferred $1,000,000.00 bond payment to BOPU.  Randy Hayes, BOPU, reviewed BOPU’s
budget preparation time frame and advised the budget as it stands has no relationship to the million
dollar deferred payment.  Mr. Brown moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr.
Spiker.  Discussion included a request to BOPU to review current watering restrictions for possible
modification, opposition to proposed rate increases, and determination/justification for proposed fee
increases.  Upon inquiry, Tim Wilson, BOPU Director, provided information on proposed increases
based on a nalysis and projec tions, degree of inflation, and a dditional factors.  Motion ca rried.
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present with the exception of Ms. Case and Mr.
Valdez voting “no”. (#3893)
“An Ordinance approving and establishing revised sewer rates, as recommended by the
Board of Public Utilities to be effective January 1, 2011.” Mr. Brown moved to approve on third and
final re ading, seconde d by Mr. Pierson.  Motion carr ied.  Voting  “yes” – a ll members of  the
governing body present with the exception of Ms. Case and Mr. Valdez voting “no”. (#3894)
“An Ordinance approving and establishing revised tap fees, system development fees, pump
station fees and other  related fees recommended by the B oard of Public Utili ties to be eff ective
January 1,  20 11.” Mr . B rown mov ed to approve on thi rd a nd fi nal r eading, s econded b y Ms.
Broyles. Comment of concern was expressed regarding proposed increases and potential hardship
on community construction and busine sses.  Motion carr ied.  Voting “yes” – all members of the
governing body present with the exception of M s. Case, Dr. Rinne and Mr. Va ldez voting “no”.
(#3895)
“An Ordinance approving and establishing revised administrative fees, service charges and
penalties as recommended by the Board of Public Utilities to be effective January 1, 2011.” Eric
Russell raised concerns with increases for administrators and raising fees that citizens can’t afford.
Randy Hayes, BOPU Administration Manager, explained administrative fees include such items as
late charges on past due accounts, NSF (non-sufficient funds) fees and other misc. fees affiliated
with cost of services provided, and stated the increase is not related to wages.  Mr. Brown moved
to approve on third and final  reading, seconded by Ms. Broy les. Comment followed on the fe e
amount pertaining to production of electronic maps with Tim Wilson, BOPU Director, advising the
cost for some of the electronic areas may be set by the intra-governmental GIS cooperative and that
he would check on this for fee consistency.  Motion failed.  Voting “yes” – Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles
and Mayor Kaysen.  Voting “no” – Ms. Ash, Ms. Case, Mr. Pierson, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Spiker and Mr.
Valdez.
“An Ordinance approving and establishing revised septic waste dump rates and  commercial
waste rates recommended by the Board of Public Utilities to be e ffective January 1, 2011.” Mr.
Brown moved to appr ove on third and f inal reading, seconded by Mr. Pierson. Motion car ried.
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present with the exception of Ms. Case and Mr.
Valdez voting “no”. (#3896)
“An Ordinance a mending Ordinanc e No. 3880 de fining the Ter ms and Conditions  of
employment for non-uniformed employees of the City of Cheyenne.” Mr. Brown moved to approve
on second reading, seconded by Ms. Broyles. Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the
governing body present. 
 
“An Or dinance amending  Ordinance No. 3881 defining the Te rms and Conditions of
employment for uniformed employees of the Cheyenne Police Department.” Mr. Brown moved to
approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Pierson. Dr. Rinne moved to a mend by striking the
word leadership following the words “city council” in Section 8 regarding furlough days, seconded
by Ms. Ash. It was noted if the amendment is approved, the section would then read as follows: The
mayor, after consulting with city council, shall have discre tionary authority to declare furlo ugh
days, if necessary, due to budget constraints.  Discussion included comments in support and non-
support of the a mendment, past discussions o n purpose of the  current wording, communication
concerns, Council members’ opportunity to provide input to the mayor, and whether “consulting”
means a meeting would be required.  It was noted that similar wording was included in the previous
ordinance a pproved o n 2  re ading re lative to non- uniformed e mployees.  Up on inq uiry, Ci tynd
Attorney Dan White clarified if the amendment was approved no formal meeting may be required,
and that the mayor would have the duty to consult with City Council members but that the ultimate
decision would be the may or’s.  Motion to amend ca rried.  V oting “yes” – a ll members of the
governing body present with the exception of Mr. Br own, Mr. Pierson and Mayor Kaysen voting
“no”.  Main motion as amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.
“An Ordinance amending the City of Cheyenne’s Sanitation fee schedule.” Eric Russell read
a statement expressing c oncerns w ith the proposed increases, the r ecycling program, affiliated
sanitation costs, and fiscal responsibility, and Ed Melko commented on concerns with fees, costs
of hauling waste to A ult, CO, life of current landfill and development status of a new landfill,
current economic situation and recycling limitation on accepted materials.  Mr. Brown moved to
approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Pierson. Mr. Brown moved to amend to reduce the fee
for 95 gal. residential recycling containers to $5.00 and 60 gal. residential recycling containers to
$3.00, seconded by Dr. Rinne.  Comment followed on the purpose of the amendment.  Motion to
amend carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.  Discussion included
Sanitation r evenue o ffsetting e xpenses f or c ity d epartments; f actors in volving p otential
establishment of a sanitation enterprise fund; recycling program; hauling waste to Ault in an effort
to extend the life of the landfill; Sanitation fee revenues, and fiscal responsibility.  Upon inquiry,
Barb Dorr, City Treasurer, clarified next fiscal year’s budget for Sanitation fees was increased by
1% overall.  Mr. Spiker moved to amend by reducing the proposed 3.4% increase to 1%, seconded
by Mr. Valdez.  Upon inquiry, Ms. Dorr clarified any excess funds received over the budgeted 1%
increase could go into the general fund reserves or be reappropriated.  She advised in the past year,
revenues did not come in as anticipated, resulting in a reappropriation.  Upon inquiry, Jim Elias,
Public Works Director, explained the process utilized for reviewing fee structures with Consumer
Price Index numbers, and clarified those areas within the proposed schedule that were unrelated to
the 3.4% increase, specifically referencing Manual Collection Waste, Yard Waste Collection (95
gal. container) and Transfer Station fees.  Ms. Dorr advised during the budget preparation process
not all individual fee a reas were increased across the board.  Mr. Elias noted the propose d 3.4%
increase for certain areas was based on the Consumer Price Index as required by the cu rrent
ordinance.  Mr. Spiker r evised his motion to amend to refle ct the areas previously noted by Mr.
Elias, as being unrelated to the proposed 3.4% increase, would remain unchanged as presented and
not be included in the proposed amendme nt.  Mr. Valdez, as sec ond to the motion to amend,
concurred with the revision.  Motion to amend as re vised failed.  V oting “yes” – Ms. Ash, Mr.
Spiker and Mr. Valdez.  Voting “no” – Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Ms. Case, Mr. Pierson, Dr. Rinne
and Mayor Kaysen.  Further discussion included: Suggestion of reducing recycling pickup times;
streamlining services fo r efficiency; minimum sanitation servicing  schedule; sanitation staffing
level, and c ommunity concerns with reducing the number of  minimum service pickups.  Upon
inquiry, Dennis Pino, Solid Waste Director, provided information on staffing levels, cross-training
efforts, and utilization of temporary help.  Main motion as amended carried.  Voti ng “yes” – all
members of the governing body present with the exception of Ms. Ash, Ms. Case, Mr. Spiker and
Mr. Valdez voting “no”. 
“An Ordinance vacating the 15-foot alley within Civitan Park, two parcels of Civitan Park
and the 60-foot right-of-way of Civitan Place between Cribbon and Dey Avenues, Arp Addition,
Fifth Filing, Cheyenne, Wyoming (located between Cribbon and Dey Avenues, south of W. Leisher
Road).”  Reporting for Public Services Committee, Ms. Ash moved to approve on second reading,
seconded by Ms. Broyles. Ms. Ash decla red a conflict of inter est (family affiliated ownership of
property in the adjacent vicinity) and vacated Council Chambers.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” –
all members of the governing body present in Council Chambers.  Following announcement of the
vote, Ms. Ash returned to Council Chambers.
“A Resolution supporting the  City  of Chey enne’s application for  Transpor tation
Enhancement A ctivities -  loc al ( TEAL) f unds for l andscaping a nd e nhancements on  the  Ea st
Pershing Blvd. Reconstruction Project and authorizing the Mayor to sign a n agreement with the
State if awarded.” Mr. Brown moved to adopt, sec onded by Ms. Broyles.  Upon inquiry, Nathan
Beauheim, Engineering Services Director, advised the grant request is affiliated with the roundabout
and provided information available on possible landscaping and enhancements that may be proposed
(ex: stamped decorative concrete), and Cindy Schneider, Grants Manager, stated the grant request
amount is $500,000.00 with an 80/20 match, and that general landscaping items could include such
things as shrubs and trees.  In response to query concerning local providers for landscaping items,
Mr. Beauheim i ndicated a bid would be issued and if Federal funds were involved an in-state
preference could not be utilized.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body
present. (#5212)
“A Resolution determining that conditions for annexation required by Wyoming Statute
§15-1-402 exist for the property described as a parcel of land being situated in a portion of Tracts
7 and 10, Dell Range Addition 2  Filing; Lot 3 Smithson View, together with a portion of Carland
Drive, Thomas Road, and College Drive, Laramie County, Wyoming (generally located north of
and adjacent to Carla Drive, between Ridge Road and College Drive.”  Ms. Ash moved to adopt,
seconded by Dr. Rinne. Motion car ried.  Voting  “yes” – a ll members of the governing body
present. (#5213)
“A Resolution increasing the level of funding placed into the Solid Waste Management
Fund for the specific purpose of providing funding to pay for solid waste tipping fees, increased
recycling costs, and paying for the lease purchase of solid waste equipment.”  Mr. Brown moved
to adopt, seconded by Mr. Valdez.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing
body present. (#5214)

Consideration of B id #M-15-10 for  a contr act between the City of Cheyenne and JTL
Group, Inc., d/b/a Knife  River of Chey enne, WY, for  the FY  2010 City  Overlay project.  J oe
Stephenson, Knife River, spoke in support of providing services under the contract regardless of
the Vandehei calming islands portion of the pr oject.  Nathan B eauheim, Engineering Services
Director, explained agenda item #32(c) (contract modification referred to Finance Committee) is
a companion to this  item and should City  Council awar d the contract and w ish to leave the
calming islands in place, the contract modification would provide the mechanism to do so.  Mr.
Brown reported there was no recommendation on the item from Finance Committee.  Mr. Brown
moved to accept the bid in an amount not to exceed $1,062,173.00,  seconded by Mr. Pierson.
Discussion included support of the c ontract per the bid doc ument; removal of c urrent calming
islands; project timing; use of  funds for othe r stree t projects; other pr ojects included in t he
bid/contract; WYDOT I–25 interchange project time frame, and delaying of other projects.  Upon
inquiry, Mr. Beauheim provided information on median heights, and Doug Vetter, City Engineer,
provided information on estimated costs affiliated with the calming islands and the intent to
improve traffic hazards and reduce maintenance costs. Further comments on the Vandehei portion
of the project included: Other areas in the City requiring street work (i.e. Snyder Ave.); delaying
the work to a  future da te; addressing drainage proble ms, and WYDOT’s pr oject in the ar ea.
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present with the exception of
Ms. Ash and Mr. Spiker voting “no”. (#5562)
[CA] Consideration of B id #M-22-10 for  a contr act between the City  of Chey enne and JTL
Group, Inc., d/b/a Knife River of Cheyenne, WY, for the 2009 Cheyenne Transit Program Bus
Stop Upgrades project.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#5563)

vehicles with police package from Spradley Barr Motors of Cheyenne, WY, for the City Police
Dept. -- referred to Finance Committee.
[CA] Consideration of Bid #R-16-10 f or fur nishing e ight ( 8) portable  multi-paramete r
defibrillators from Physio-Control, Inc. of Redmond, WA for the City  of Chey enne Fire and
Rescue  Department -- referred to Finance Committee. 
Announcement was made  of the re -appointments of J o Butler , Robert Kana rd, B ebe
Molway, Carol Matteson Pascal, Terry Livingston, Abby Yenco, Jay Guerin, Dick Krahenbuhl,
Bradley Ober g, Doyle Tra vis, Tom DeHoff , Nanc y Olson and Rick Parish to the Gre enway
Advisory Committee to be confirmed June 28, 2010. 
Announcement was made of the appointment of Laura Page to the Greenway Advisory
Committee to be confirmed June 28, 2010. 
Announcement was made of a Public Meeting to be held June 28, 2010, at 6:00 p.m. in
City Council Chambe rs, 2101 O'Neil, for the purpose of receiving public co mment o n the
submission of a 5307 Federal Grant to fund transportation services by Cheyenne Transit for FY
2011. 
Under other City  business, Frank Cole, deve loper, commented on pre vious discussion
involving the approval of Vouchers with reference to the towing of vehicles affiliated with street
work projects and past practice of street signage serving as notification to homeowners; stated his
belief that any amendments to the budget ordinance should be by ordinance and not resolution;
expressed concern with the Consent Agenda process, and stated, relative to agenda item discussion
involving the Overlay Project contract awarded to Knife River, that years ago Council reviewed
bid items prior to advertising  for bids. Also under  other business, in re sponse to Mr. Cole’s
previous comments re lative to the Consent Agenda process, it was note d that members of  the
public can request an item be removed from the Consent Agenda by contacting a Council member.
Also under other business, concern was expressed regarding the complaint driven process
for enforcement of zoning sign violations, and sprinklers operating at Sun Valley park during rain
and the ability to monitor operational time frames.
It was announced the public ribbon cutting for the grand opening of the David R. Romero
Park would be held Thursday, June 17 at 1:30 p.m.  Rick Parish, Parks and Recreation Director,
provided brief information on the ribbon cutting pr ogram and participants with Mayor Kaysen
noting the park is still in its first phase of development. 
There being no further  business to come bef ore the governing body, the meeting  was
officially adjourned at 8:50 p.m. 
 
 Submitted by,
 
 
 
 Carol Intlekofer, MMC
 City Clerk
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request.

 June 24, 2010

MEETING: City Council | DATE: 2010-06-28 | CLIP_ID: 160 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 160

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
June 28, 2010
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning
at 6 :00 p .m. i n C ouncil Chambers.  P resent w ere: M AYOR -  R ichard L.  K aysen; C OUNCIL
MEMBERS - Ms. Ash, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Ms. Case, Mr. Collins, Mr. Pierson, Dr. Rinne, Mr.
Spiker and Mr. Valdez.  Also present: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk; Barb Dorr, City Treasurer, and
Dan White, City Attorney.  The pledge of allegiance was recited.
Recognition of 2010 Wyoming Association of Municipalities Innovation Awards to the City
of Cheyenne.  Mayor Kaysen advised the City was recognized with the following innovation awards
recently at the Wyoming Association of Municipalities summer convention:  Fire and Rescue Dept.,
Emergency Services Rehabilitation Unit; Sanitation Zonar GPS System; Parks and Recreation Dept.,
David R. Romero Youth Ac tivity and Community  Center, a nd Metropolitan Tra nsportation
Organization, Transportation Safety Implementation Effort and Wayfinding Plan.  Mayor Kaysen
recognized individual st aff members whose  depar tments rece ived awa rds, and e xpressed
appreciation to all staff who assisted with the program areas that had been recognized by WAM.
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA]  are considered to be
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its
normal sequence on the agenda.)  Upon request by Ms. Case, and pursuant to support received by
Ms. Broyles and Mr. Pierson, agenda items #23 and #24  were removed from the Consent Agenda.
Comment on removal of agenda item #25 followed with Mayor Kaysen advising the item was not
eligible to be removed from the Consent Agenda, pursuant to approved procedures by Council, as
it was a referral to committee.  During further comment, Mr. Brown raised a point of order stating
the Mayor had already ruled that the item c ould not be removed from the Consent Agenda.  Mr.
Collins moved to approve  remaining Consent A genda items, seconde d by Mr. B rown. Motion
carried. Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception of Ms. Case voting
“no”.
[CA] Minutes from Reg ular Meeting of the Gover ning Body on June 14, 2010.  Motion (per
Consent Agenda) carried.
[CA] Minutes from Vouchers Meeting of the Gove rning Body on June 21, 2010.  Motion (per
Consent Agenda) carried.
Mr. Brown declared a conflict of interest (business interest) due to a  voucher payable to
Ameri-Tech HVAC Services, a nd vacated Council Chambers.  Mr. Pierson moved to a pprove
payment of vouchers a s p resented, se conded b y Mr . V aldez. U pon in quiry, D oug Vetter, City
Engineer, provided information concerning a voucher payable to AVI Engineering for professional
services for Henderson Basin Project, stating services being provided is follow-up to the Henderson
project which required re-mapping of area flood improvements.  Upon inquiry involving a voucher
payable to the Laramie County Treasurer for property tax due on the former Mary’s Bake Shoppe
site, now owned by the City, Dan White, City Attorney, advised he would research the C ity’s
exempt st atus requir ements re lative to proper ty tax assessments.  Ci ty Trea surer Ba rb Dorr
indicated, due to timeliness involved with the payment, a manual check had been issued.  Motion
carried.  Vo ting “ yes” – a ll m embers of  the  g overning body present in Co uncil Ch ambers.
Following announcement of the vote, Mr. Brown returned to Council Chambers.
Mayor Kaysen opened a Public Meeting for the purpose of receiving public comments for
the submission of a 5307 Federal Grant to fund transportation services by Cheyenne Transit for
FY2011.   Joe Dougherty, Cheyenne Transit Director, advised the grant application is a request for
operating and capitol assistance to help fund the Transit program; provided a hand-out (on file with
City Clerk’s office) referencing the budget for the grant, noting the funding level available is less
than last year, and provided program operational information.  There being no further comments or
input and purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor Kaysen closed the hearing.
“An Or dinance amen ding Ordinanc e No. 3880 de fining the terms and c onditions of
employment for non-uniformed employees of the City of Cheyenne.” Reporting for Fi nance
Committee, Mr. Brown moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Valdez.  Dr.
Rinne moved to amend Section 7, referencing furlough days, by deleting the wor d leadership
following the words “city council,” seconded by Ms. Ash. Comments followed on the purpose of
the amendment, which would provide that the Mayor consult with all City Council members if
furlough days are proposed.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing
body with the exception of Mr. B rown and Mr. Pierson voting “ no”.  Main motion as amende d
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.  (#3897)
“An Ordinance amending Ordinance No. 3881 defining the terms and conditions of
employment for uniformed employees of the Cheyenne Police Department.”  Mr. Brown moved to
approve on third and final  reading, seconded by Mr. Valdez. It was clarified the word leadership
had been previously deleted from the section involving furlough days pursuant to an amendment
approved at the June 14  meeting.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governingth
body. (#3898)
“An Ordinance amending the City  of Chey enne’s Sanitation fee sc hedule.” M r. Brown
moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Pierson.  Mr. Brown moved to amend
by substitut e, noting the amendme nt would reduc e the r ate increase to 1% with the following
exceptions: leaving the recycle rates as approved through the previous substitute, and leaving the
following rates as originally proposed: Residential Manual Collection fee, $20.00; Transfer Station
Dumping Residential Fee, $10.00; Yard Waste Collection Monthly Fee 95 Gallon, $6.00 and Yard
Waste Collection Monthly Fee for Residential and Commercial 1.5 CY fee, $15.00, seconded by Dr.
Rinne.  Comments follow ed on increasing the rates pursuant to the Consumer Price  Index to be
consistent with other sanitation fe e increases of 3.4%, a pproval of ba lanced budget with a 1%
increase, monthly difference between a 3.4% increase ($.54) and 1% increase ($.16), effect of rate
increases on low income residents, incremental and more frequent increases vs. larger non-frequent
increases, and estimated FY2011 budget revenues/expenditures.  Up on inquiry, Barb Dorr, City
Treasurer, advised unknown e xpenses during a fiscal year could af fect budget figures, citing an
example involving a previous budget, following approval, involving increases in fuel costs for Fleet
Maintenance.  During discussion, City Clerk Carol Intlekofer reviewed the motions on the floor.
Motion to amend by substitute carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the
exception of Mr. Brown and Mr. Pierson voting “no.”  Ma in motion as amended by substitute
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception of Mr. Brown, Ms.
Case and Mayor Kaysen voting “no”. (#3899)
“An Ordinance vacating the 15-foot alley within Civitan Park, two parcels of Civitan Park
and the 60-foot right-of-way of Civitan Place between Cribbon and Dey Avenues, Arp Addition,
Fifth Filing, Cheyenne, Wyoming (located between Cribbon and Dey Avenues, south of W. Leisher
Road).”  Reporting for Public Services Committee, Mr. Collins moved to approve on third and final
reading, se conded by  Ms. Broy les.  Ms. Ash decla red a  conflict of interes t (family aff iliated
ownership of property in the adjacent vicinity), and vacated Council Chambers.  Motion car ried.
Voting “ yes” – a ll members of the g overning body present in Council C hambers.  Following
announcement of the vote, Ms. Ash returned to Council Chambers. (#3900)
“An Ordinance repealing Chapter 5.72, Public Transport, in its entirety and re -creating
Chapter 5.72, Public Transportation, of Title 5, Business Licenses and Regulations, of the Cheyenne

Contract Modification No. 1 to Contract #5562 between JTL Group dba Knife River for
the FY2010 City Overlay Project to leave  existing traffic calming islands in place, de lete new
traffic safety islands from contract, place storm sewer in new alignment adding one 5 ft diameter
storm sewer manhole to avoid conflicts with existing islands (2007-2010 1% Sales and Use Tax).
Mr. Brown reported there was no recommendation on the item from F inance Committee.  Mr.
Collins moved to approve, seconded by Ms. Ash.  Di scussion included the following topics:
Accident traffic st udy relative to calming  island are as on Va ndehei; cost to re move exist ing
islands; comments and input received from the public by Council members regarding the islands;
traffic maneuvering and pedestrian safety concerns, and law enforcement of speed limits in lieu
of calming islands.  Upon in quiry, Tom Mason, MPO Direc tor, reviewed traffic data history
involving speeds in the area, and noted the islands were originally installed at the request of the
neighborhood.  Mr. Mason also reviewed the use of center medians instead of calming islands to
keep speeds down, and explained the purpose and general provisions of the neighborhood traffic
management program.  Discussion continued and included: Truck traffic off I-25 as a reason for
the original installation of calming  is lands on Va ndehei, public mee tings held as part of  the
planning process involving the decision to implement the calming islands, and other needed street
repair projects and prioritization of same. Upon inquiry whether a modification could be made to
another area of the original contract, unrelated to the calming islands modification item on the
agenda, City Attorney Dan White advised this would require preparation of a separate contract
modification.  Continuing discussion followed.  Pursuant to questions and comments raised during
discussion, Mayor Kaysen provided information on the proposed remedy to address any increase
in traffic speeds if the current islands are removed (i.e. median re-design pursuant to the original
contract without the modification), clarified his [election] campaign promise was only to review
the calming island issue, and a dvised of input he ha d received from the public conce rning the
islands.  Motion failed.  Voting “ yes” – Ms. Ash, Ms. B royles, Mr. Collins and Mr. Spiker.
Voting “no” – Mr. Brown, Ms. Case, Mr. Pierson, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Valdez and Mayor Kaysen.
