Announcement was made that due to the 4th of July holiday on Monday, July 5, 2010, the
following meetings have been rescheduled for Tuesday, July 6, 2010: Finance Committee at 11:00
a.m., Ro om 1 22;  r egular V ouchers meeting of  the  Go verning B ody a t N oon, City  Cou ncil
Chambers, and Public Services Committee at 12:15 p.m., Room 122, Municipal Building, 2101
O’Neil Ave., Cheyenne, WY.
Announcement was made of a Calcutta to be held Saturday, July 31, 2010 beginning at
5:30 p.m. at the VIP/Sponsor Tent located in Frontier Park in conjunction with the 2010 Cheyenne
Frontier Days Rodeo. Proceeds to benefit Cheyenne area charities.
Announcement was made of a Calcutta to be held Friday, August 27, 2010 beginning at
5:30 p.m. at the Old West Museum, 4610 Carey Avenue, in conjunction with Laramie County
Republican Party Fall fundraiser. Proceeds to benefit Laramie County Republican Party.
Under other business:
Lucinda Schmid expressed concerns with proposed expansion plans by Cheyenne Regional
Medical Center into the Lakeview and Rainsford Historic Districts and submitted a petition (on
file with City  Clerk’s off ice) signed by interested individuals re questing City communication
assistance with the hospital.  Mayor Kaysen advised the City is aware of expansion plans by the
Medical Center and Doug Vetter, City Engineer, stated no formal plans have been submitted yet
to the City.  I t was noted the City process will involve the opportunity for public input.  Ms.
Schmidt expressed that she feels there has been a lack of communication between the hospital and
the neighborhood.  Jan Nelson-Schroll also expressed co ncerns with the expansion plans and
commented on community planning and City Council’s role, encouraging the Council to be pre-
active in promoting vital neighborhoods; light and noise pollution and preserving historic districts.
Police Chief Brian Kozak recognized the upcoming Neighborhood Night Out community
event scheduled for July 13, 2010; advised the graffitti abatement program began June 28, 2010,
and stated a drug recognition class for parents was scheduled for June 30, 2010, 6:00 p.m. at the
Cheyenne Police Department. Upon inquiry later under other business, Chief Kozak advised 36
tickets were issued last month for violation of the cell phone usage while driving ordinance.
Comment was made concerning employee diligence when mowing around signs during
maintenance operations.
City Attorney Dan White requested the governing body meet in executive session (legal
briefing) on new litigation concerning the Southwest Drive annexation, and a potential complaint
that may be filed by the City against the Laramie County Commissioners regarding a re-platting
issue.  Mr. Pierson moved to meet in executive session, seconded by Mr. Valdez.  Motion carried.
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.  Mayor Kaysen announced members would
meet in  e xecutive se ssion in C ouncil Chambers, n ot o pen to  the  pu blic; r equested th e City
Attorney, City Clerk, and other staff as requested by the City Attorney, be present at the executive
session, and stated ther e would b e no action taken by  the g overning body as a r esult of the
executive session, and that the regular City Council meeting would officially conclude at the close
of the executive session.  (Clerk’s Note: Executive session convened at 8:10 p.m. following a brief
recess.)
There being no further  business to come bef ore the governing body prior to executive
session, the meeting  was officially adjourned at 8:27 p.m. following c losure of the  executive
session.
 
 Submitted by,
 
 
 
 Carol Intlekofer, MMC
 City Clerk
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request.

MEETING: City Council | DATE: 2010-07-12 | CLIP_ID: 162 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 162

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
July 12, 2010
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning
at 6 :00 p .m. i n C ouncil Chambers.  P resent w ere: M AYOR -  R ichard L.  K aysen; C OUNCIL
MEMBERS - Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Ms. Case, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Spiker and Mr. Valdez.  Absent:
Ms. Ash, Mr. Collins and Mr. Pierson.  Also present: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk; Barb Dorr, City
Treasurer, and Dan White, City Attorney.  The pledge of allegiance was recited.
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA]  are considered to be
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its
normal sequence on the agenda.)  Upon request by Mr. Brown, and pursuant to support received by
Ms. Broyles and Mr. Spiker agenda items #19(a) and (b)  were removed from the Consent Agenda.
Mr. Brown  moved to approve remaining Consent Agenda items, seconded by Mr. Spiker. Motion
carried. Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.
[CA] Minutes from Reg ular Meeting of the Gover ning Body on J une 28, 20 10.  Motion (per
Consent Agenda) carried.
[CA] Minutes from Vouchers Meeting of the Gover ning Body on J uly 6, 2010.  Motion (per
Consent Agenda) carried.
Mr. Brown declared a conflict of interest (business interest) due to a  voucher payable to
Ameri-Tech H VAC Service s, and vac ated Council Chamber s.  Mr. Va ldez moved to approve
payment of vouchers as presented, seconded by Mr. Spiker.  Upon inquiry, City Treasurer Barb Dorr
reviewed a voucher payable to All-Irwin Corporation ($285.13), sand for Airport Golf Course, and
explained payment of sales tax was included as other states do not have to recognize Wyoming’s
state sales tax exemption for municipalities.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the
governing body present in Council Chambers.  Following announcement of the vote, Mr. B rown
returned to Council Chambers.
“An Ordinance repealing Chapter 5.72, Pub lic Transport, in it s entirety and re -creating
Chapter 5.72, Public Transportation, of Title 5, Business Licenses and Regulations, of the Cheyenne
City Code.” Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. Brown moved to approve on second reading,
seconded by  Mr. Spiker.  Comments fol lowed regarding Section 5.72.010 C. which provides
authorization for the police chief to recommend denial of an application within a one year time
frame of an applicant being convicted of various offenses, and costs associated with fingerprinting
and background checks.  Upon inquiry, Lt. Mark Munari, Cheyenne Police Dept., advised research
conducted reflected the one year review time frame was comparable with what other cities have in
place, and provided information on fees assessed per state law for applicant background checks.  Mr.
Spiker moved to amend Section 5.72.030 D. to change the word “may” to “shall”, seconded by Ms.
Broyles, which would enable the section to read as follows: “The chief of police SHALL impose
a reasonable fee for costs assoc iated with any  criminal or other  background investigation of an
applicant, including the costs of fingerprinting.”  Lt. Munari advised the same language appears in
Section 5.72.050 C.  Mr. Spiker, with concurrence by Ms. Broyles as second to the motion, revised
the motion to include the same change to Section 5.72.050 C. and any other areas in the ordinance
pertaining to the same topic and fee.  Donald James Perot McNeil commented that the word “shall”
is more forc eful than “ may”.  Discussion followed on providing  discretionary authority for the
police chief through the more pe rmissible term “may ”, and c onsistency in assessing  a fe e for
fingerprinting and background checks.  Following inquiry to Mr. Spiker, as maker of the motion,
Mayor Kaysen clarified the specific ordinance sections proposed for change (i.e. striking “may” and
inserting “shall”) p ursuant t o th e mot ion to a mend were Se ction 5 .72.030 D . a nd 5. 72.050 C.
Motion to amend car ried.  Vo ting “yes” – all members of the g overning body present with the
exception of Ms. Case voting “no”.  Upon inquiry, City Attorney Dan White clarified the current
smoking in public places ordinance prohibits smoking in public transpor tation conveyances, and
advised the inclusion of wording in the ordinance pertaining to the prohibition of cell phone use
while operating a conveyance was to make it perfectly clear that drivers can not conduct business
through use of a cell phone while driving unless they are in compliance with city code.  Main motion
as amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present with the exception
of Ms. Case voting “no”. 
“An Or dinance annexing to the City  of Chey enne, Wyoming, a par cel of  land being
situated in a portion of tracts 7 and 10, Dell Rang e Addition, 2  Filing; Lot 3 Smithson View,nd
together w ith a  p ortion o f C arla D rive, T homas R oad and C ollege D rive, L aramie C ounty,
Wyoming (generally located nor th of and adjacent to Carla Dr ive, between Ridge Road a nd
College Drive).” Reporting for Public Services Committee, Dr. Rinne moved to approve on second
reading, seconded by Mr. Brown. Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing
body present. 
“An Ordinance amending Title 17, Zoning, Chapter 17.08, Zoning Districts Established
and Section 17.08.040 Cheyenne City map of the Cheyenne City Code, by changing the zoning
classification from P Public District to HR-2 Hig h Density-Residential Developing District for

classification from P-Publi c, MR-1-Me dium Res idential Established and L I-Light I ndustrial
Districts to LI-Light Industrial District for BOPU Subdivision, a replat of portions of Blocks 98, 99,
and 151; all of Blocks 149 and 150; and va cated streets and alleyways, Original City, Cheyenne,
Wyoming (located at the northwest corner of the intersection of Snyder Avenue and W. 24th Street)”
-- referred to Public Services Committee (sponsor - Ms. Ash).
“An Ordinance amending Title 17, Zoning, Chapter 17.08, Zoning Districts Established and
Section 17.08.040 Chey enne City  map of the Chey enne City  Code, by  chang ing the zoning
classification from P Publ ic District to MUR Mix ed Use-With Residential Emphasis District for
Garey Addition, 2  Filing, a replat of a portion of Lot 2 and all of Lots 3 and 4, Garey Addition,nd
Cheyenne, Wyoming (located north of and adjacent to W. Fox Farm Road, West of Walterscheid
Boulevard)” -- referred to Public Services Committee (sponsor - Ms. Ash).
“A Resolution supporting the  continuation of the Optional One Percent Sales Tax for
Calendar Years 2011 through 2014 and pledging that all revenues from this tax will be used f or
projects describe d in this resolution.” Speaking  in su pport of the r esolution and funding  for
community Human Services agencies were: Connie Sloan-Cathcart, United Way of Laramie County;
Carol Matteson Pascal; Judith Johnston, Foster Grandparent program; Ken Burke, Laramie County
Centralized Pharmacy; Lynne Weidel, Chey enne Community Health and Wellness  Center, and
Sharon Benson, Meals on W heels.  Comments ref erenced the history of whe n Human Services
agencies were included in 5  penny sales tax funding; community partnership and goals; Humanth
Services Advisory Committee’s role; funding amount allocated to agencies; support for the joint
application and review process involving the City/County and United Way; use of funding received
to match federal and state dollars for programs, and funding continuation to assist with increased
program usage. Joe Morelli questioned use of overage monies, and prioritization of road projects,
specifically Christensen Road.  Dona ld James Perot McNeil spoke in opposition to t he 5  pennyth
sales tax ballot question in general. Karolyn Middleton questioned funding for the Christensen Road
overpass project relative to the improvement benefitting private developers, and Pacomio Sandoval
expressed concern with use, including the approval process, of overage monies.  Mr. Brown moved
to adopt, s econded by Mr. Valdez.  M r. Brown moved to amend by substitute resolution which
amends page 2, Sections #7(d) and #7(e), Street and Road Maintenance and Construction projects,
by reversing th e p rioritization o rder to  r eflect # 7(e) ( Design a nd e nvironmental c learance f or
Christensen Rd. overpass) as #4 and #7(d) (Replace culverts at 19th St. and Crow Creek and Hilltop
over Dry Creek) as #5, seconded by Ms. Broyles.  Ms. Case raised a point of clarification regarding
the public comment proc ess with May or Ka ysen advising  he would di vert from the normal
procedure if the public has further questions/comments.  Discussion topics included the purpose for
the proposed change in the priority listing; anticipated revenue level, which may not be sufficient
to f und all of the priority items; Christensen Rd. overpass project, which involves desig n, not
construction, to have a  “shovel re ady” proje ct when f unding is a vailable; culve rt repla cement
project, and future fire station planned for the Christensen Rd. area and response time the overpass
would provide.  John Volk, Campstool Land Co., provided information on development of land in
the Christensen Rd. area, noting land donations for rights-of-way and infrastructure assistance that
have been provided by the company; spoke in favor of the Christiansen Rd. overpass work that will
assist with ve hicular connection to that par t of the City , and future  commercial recruiting and
development in the ar ea, a nd commented on the importa nce of  desig n-ready projec ts, lim ited
funding sources available, growth in the eastern part of the community and economic development.
Upon inquiry, Vicki Nemecek, Asst. Public Works Director, explained the optional one percent sales
tax revenue collection process and time frame, and stated the amount estimated to be collected for
the next 4 year term is $40,000,000.  She further explained the definition of overage monies (funds
collected that exceed the estimated amount), noting the resolution specifies that 80% of any overage
funds is to be utilized for street and road maintenance and construction projects as proposed in the
resolution, and provided information on projects that will not be funded unless overage funds are
received.  Conce rn with public input and timing for  input was expressed by Mr. Morelli, and
concerns with funding the Christensen Rd. overpass project were conveyed by Mr. Morelli and Mr.
McNeil.  Ms. Pascal questioned the percentage distribution for use of overage monies, if received.
Ms. Nemecek explained the public meeting and governing body approval process involving use of
overage funds.  Motion to amend by substitute carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing
body present.  Mr. Spiker moved to amend the  secon d “Be It Further Resolved” paragraph as
follows: “Excess monies shall be distributed as follows: 80% shall be allocated to Street and Road
Maintenance and Construction projects listed on page 2 of this resolution; 10% shall be distributed
to Human Services Agencies in accordance with recommendations made by the Human Services
Advisory Council and approved by the governing body; and the remaining 10% of excess monies
shall be spent for such purposes, as may be approved by the governing body following a public
hearing,” seconded by Ms. Broyles.  Mr. McNeil commented in opposition of the sales tax ballot
question.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Burke provided information on the ty pe of medications distributed
through the Laramie County Centralized Pharmacy program, and Ms. Johnston clarified volunteers,
who must meet Federal guidelines, for the Foster Grandparent program receive a Federal stipend
amount for personal use.  Discussion on the proposed amendment involved comment that Human
Services agencies will not benefit from the amendment unless overage monies are received, history
of 1% sa les tax funding for  Human Servic es agencies since 1986, a nd suggestion that proposed
funding for Street and Road Maintenance and Construction be reduced by $400,000 and inserted in
the Human Ser vices agencies line item.  Mr. Spi ker withdrew his motion to amend. Dr. Rinne
moved to amend to redistribute $400,000 from the propose d Street and Road Ma intenance and
Construction line item and to the line item for Human Service agencies, and in the second “Be It
Further Resolved”  para graph to chang e 80% of  excess monies allocate d to Street and Road
Maintenance and Construction to 90%, leaving the remaining10% of excess monies to be spent for
such purposes as may be approved by the governing body following a public hearing, seconded by
Mr. Spiker.  Ms. Sloan-Cathca rt spoke in favor of the motion to amend, a nd provided additional
information on the stipend received by volunteers involved with certain programs.  Upon inquiry
by Mayor Kaysen, Ms. Br oyles, as sec ond to the motion to amend as pre viously made by  Mr.
Spiker, conc urred with Mr. Spiker’s re quest to withd raw the motion.  Ms. Case ra ised a
parliamentary procedural question involving the proper process to be followed under Robert’s Rules
of Order (RROO) r elative to wit hdrawing a motion after it “be longs to the body .”  F ollowing
discussion on parliamentary procedures, Mayor Kaysen indicated the governing body could vote on
the previous motion offered by Mr. Spiker, and stated the wording of the motion.  Upon inquiry,
City Attorney Dan White, referenced RROO and advised before a motion has been stated by the
chair, it is the property of the mover , who ca n withdraw the  motion; i f the c hair has stated the
question then it is the property  of the Council and sho uld be voted upon prior to ac ting on a
secondary motion as offered by Dr. Rinne. He offered to research RROO further if desired.  Mayor
Kaysen recessed the me eting for 10 minutes to allow fur ther procedural review by Mr. White.
Mayor Kaysen reconvened the meeting at 7:55 p.m. and stated Mr. Spiker’s motion t o amend
remained on the floor.  Mr. Spiker requested his motion be withdrawn.  Mayor Kaysen questioned
whether there was any objection by City Council members regarding the request to withdraw the
motion and, upon no objection being voiced, stated the motion was being withdrawn.  Dr. Rinne
reiterated his previous motion to amend to redistribute $400,000 from the proposed Street and Road
Maintenance and Construction line item and move the amount to the line item for Human Services
agencies, and in the second “Be It Further Resolved” paragraph to change 80% of excess monies
allocated to Street and Road Maintenance and Construction to 90%, leaving the remaining10% of
excess monies to be spent for such purposes as may be approved by the governing body following
a public hea ring, seconded by Mr. Spiker.  Discussion followed on the  intent and effect of the
motion to amend with regard to community street and road projects and amount of overage funds
that would be required to replenish the amount proposed to be removed from that line item; status
of the West Pershing, west of Snyder Avenue, project; intent of the resolution, as initially presented,
to use 80% of funding for street and road projects, and leaving monies earmarked for projects as is.
During discussion, Jim Elias, Public Works Director, and Ms. Nemecek provided information on
the estimated overage amount that would need to be collected to replenish the line item if it were
reduced, and Doug Vetter, City Engineer, advised the West Pershing project is being designed by
WYDOT with construction anticipated to begin in 2012.  Motion to amend failed. Voting “yes” –
Dr. Rinne and Mr . Spiker.  Voting  “no” – Mr . Brown, Ms. Broyles, Ms. Case, Mr. Va ldez and
Mayor Ka ysen.  Mr. Spiker moved to a mend the sec ond “B e I t Furthe r Resolved”  para graph
concerning excess monies t o read  “Excess monies shall be distributed a s follows: 80% shall be
allocated to  Str eet a nd Roa d M aintenance a nd Con struction p rojects l isted on page 2 of this
resolution; 10 % s hall be distributed to  Hu man Se rvices a gencies in  a ccordance wi th
recommendations made by the Human Services Advisory Council and approved by the governing
body; and the remaining 10% of excess monies shall be spent for such purposes, as may be approved
by the governing body following a public hearing,” seconded by Mr. Valdez. Mr. McNeil stated he
did not see the necessity of the amendment.  Upon inquiry, Ms. Nemecek reviewed the purpose of
removing revolv ing loan fund lang uage fr om the second “ Be I t Furthe r Resolved”  para graph,
indicating use of revolving loan funds has been approved by a separate  resolution and is coordinated
through Urban Planning, as well as removal of wording in the same paragraph relative to excess
funds that may be used to repair City facilities, explaining this is already included in individual line
items. Comments followed regarding the motion on the floor vs. removal of additional language as
discussed by Ms. Nemecek.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing
body present with the exception of Mr. Brown and Ms. Case voting “no”.  U pon inquiry, Ms.
Nemecek advised an additional amendment, pursuant to her previous comments, would serve to
“clean up” language in the second “Be It Further Resolved” paragraph and that the proposed change
in wording was pursuant to recommendation by the City Attorney.  Mr. Spiker moved to amend by
striking the following sentences at the end of the second “Be It Further Resolved” paragraph: “This
portion of excess funds may also be used to c onstruct and r epair city facilities and e stablish a
revolving loan fund to assist property owners in repairing or building infrastructure (curb, gutter,
sidewalk, streets, utility connections, etc.).  Appropriation of the remaining 20% of excess interest
or overage funds requires a public meeting and approval of the governing body.”, seconded by Mr.
Valdez.  City Clerk Carol Intlekofer requested clarification of the wording of the motion with regard
to the previous amendment to the paragraph. Ms. Nemecek reviewed the intended wording for the
paragraph.  Mr. Spiker clarified intent of  his motion to a mend would result in t he f ollowing
paragraph wording in its entirety:  “Be It Further Resolved that all funds realized from the 2011-
2014 optional one percent sales tax shall be spent to complete one percent sales tax projects.  Funds
collected from the optional one percent sales tax which exceed the estimate of $40,000,000 (overage
funds) and interest earned on one percent sales tax fund deposits which exceed administrative costs
(excess interest funds) are for the purposes of this resolution collectively described as  ‘exc ess
monies’.  Excess monies shall be distributed as follows: 80% shall be allocated to Street and Road
Maintenance and Construction projects listed on page 2 of this resolution; 10% shall be distributed
to Human Services agencies in accordance with recommendations made by the Human Ser vices
Advisory Council and approved by the governing body; and the remaining 10% of excess monies
shall be spent for such purposes, as may be approved by the governing body following a pu blic
hearing.”  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.
Comment was made that c ertain items under #7 on the Stree t and Road Maintena nce a nd
Construction priority listing will be unfunded if overage revenues are not received, and, if the items
are listed, the public may perceive these as projects to be completed.  Dr. Rinne moved to delete
items #7(c)(d) and (e) from the priority listing.  Motion died due to lack of a second.  Main motion
as amended three times carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present. (#5224)

 Consideration of Bid #R-14-10 for furnishing one new Wheel Excavator for the City  of

Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held Monday, July 26, 2010 at 6:00 p.m.
in City Council Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, WY to receive public comments on the
proposed trade of real properties owned by the City of Cheyenne, being two parcels of land which
comprised the former Civitan Park, the north half of the right-of-way for Civitan Place, a platted but
unbuilt street, and the right-of-way for a platted, but unbuilt, alley, all of which are located in Arp
Addition, 5  Filing, generally located south of West Leisher Road between Cribbon Avenue andth
Dey Avenue, and all of w hich were vacated by t he Governing Body of the City  of Chey enne
pursuant to Ordinance No. 3900, approved June 28, 2010, and together consisting of approximately
45,519 square feet +/- (estimated value of $80,000.00), to Laramie County School District No. 1 for
real property  owned by  L aramie County  School District No. 1 described as a strip of land
approximately 126 ft. wide from east to west and approximately 398 ft. long from north to south,
consisting of approximately 50,148 square feet +/- (estimated value of $80,000.00), situated in the
eastern part of Sunrise Elementary School site within Lot 1, Block 1, Sun Valley School Addition,
Cheyenne, WY generally located on the north side of East 12  St, adjacent to Sun Valley Park.th
Under other business:
Bryan Grzegorczyk, President, Laramie County Liquor Association, provided information
on a taxi ride program that will be implemented during this year’s Cheyenne Frontier Days, stating
a liquor representative is purchasing 1,000 free taxi rides to assist people in being transported safely
to/from liquor establishments.  He advise d advertising of the program will be conducted.  Mayor
Kaysen expressed appreciation to the Association for program efforts involving residents’ safety.
Mayor Kaysen reminded the public the first Cheyenne Neighborhood Night Out event will
be Tu esday e vening, July  13 , 2010 a t di fferent l ocations thr oughout t he c ommunity, a nd a lso
advised a public forum would be held Wednesday, July 14, 2010 beginning at 6:00 p.m., Council
Chambers, regarding the issue of trees in Lions Park and air traffic involving Cheyenne Regional
Airport.
There being  no further  business to come before the g overning body , Mayor Ka ysen
adjourned the meeting at 8:48 p.m.

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
July 26, 2010
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning
at 6 :00 p .m. i n C ouncil Chambers.  P resent w ere: M AYOR -  R ichard L.  K aysen; C OUNCIL
MEMBERS - Ms. Ash, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Ms. Case, Mr. Collins, Mr. Pierson, Dr. Rinne, Mr.
Spiker and Mr. Valdez.  Also present: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk; Barb Dorr, City Treasurer, and
Dan White, City Attorney.  The pledge of allegiance was recited.
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA]  are considered to be
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its
normal sequence on the agenda.)   Mr. Spiker  moved to approve Consent Agenda items, seconded
by Mr. Brown. Motion carried. Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.
[CA] Minutes fr om Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on July 12, 2010.  Motion (per
Consent Agenda) carried.
[CA] Minutes from Vouche rs Meeting of the Gover ning Body on July 19, 2010.  Motion (per
Consent Agenda) carried.
Mr. Valdez and Mr. Collins each declared a conflict of interest (business interest in vouchers
payable to radio station KYOY and Bicycle Station, Inc. respectively).  Mr. Valdez vacated Council
Chambers.  Mr. Brown  mo ved to approve payment of vouchers as presented, seconded by Mr.
Spiker.  Upon inquiry , City Treasurer Barb Dorr reviewed information available on a vouc her
payable to Alco Mobile Storage LLC ($150.00), rent for temporary storage unit at Depot Plaza, and
Mayor Kaysen advised information would be provided relative to completion of the Depot Plaza
Operations building and termination of storage rentals. Upon inquiry regarding a voucher payable
to American Medical Response ($522.50), ambulance services, Todd Feezer, Asst. Director, Parks
and Recreation Dept., expressed his belief that the contract agreement with the event organizer for
use of the Taco John’s Events Center for the MMA Fight required an on-site ambulance during the
event, noting that contra ct (lease of the f acility) revenue off-sets the expense.  Motion carr ied.
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present in Council Chambers with the exception
of Mr. Collins, who did not vote.  Following announcement of the vote, Mr. Valdez returned to
Council Chambers.
Mayor Kaysen opened a public hearing to receive comments and input on the proposed trade
of real properties owned by the City of Cheyenne, being two parcels of land which comprised the
former Civitan Park, the north half of the right-of-way for Civitan Place, a platted but unbuilt street,
and the right-of-way for a platted, but unbuilt, alley, all of which are located in Arp Addition, 5 th
Filing, generally located south of West Leisher Road between Cribbon Avenue and Dey Avenue,
and a ll o f w hich w ere va cated by  the Governing B ody of  the  City  of  Che yenne pu rsuant t o
Ordinance No. 3900, a pproved June 28 , 2010, and tog ether consisting of  approximately 45,519
square feet +/- (estimated value of $80,000.00), to Laramie County School District No. 1 for real
property owned by Laramie County School District No. 1 described as a strip of land approximately
126 ft. wide fr om east to wes t and approximately 398 ft. long  from north to south, consisting of
approximately 50,148 square feet +/- (estimated value of $80,000.00), situated in the eastern part
of Sunrise Elementary School site within Lot 1, Block 1, Sun Valley School Addition, Cheyenne,
WY generally located on the north side of East 12  St, adjacent to Sun Valley Park.  Todd Feezer,th
Asst. Director, Parks and Recreation Dept., referenced two maps he displayed reflecting the parcels
involved in the property trade, which will benefit the new Goins Elementary school building and
new park land for the City.  There being no further comments or input and purpose of the hearing
having been met, Mayor Kaysen closed the hearing.
“An Ordinance repealing Chapter 5.72, Public Transport, in its entirety and re -creating
Chapter 5.72, Public Transportation, of Title 5, Business Licenses and Regulations, of the Cheyenne
City Code.” Joe Elkins, owner, B.C. Cab, expressed concerns with several sections of the proposed
ordinance and discussed leasing of public transportation conveyances to drivers; proof of vehicle
insurance; carrying or delivering of alcoholic or malt beverages; metered vs. non-metered tracking
relative to daily trip sheet requirements, and offering cab companies the option to have a distinctive
color scheme (logo) similar to the option provided to limousine and carriage service providers.  Mr.
Elkins further c ommented on lack of  enforcement of curr ent code and state statutes involving
business practices by other c ab companies that he fe lt were in violation of law.  Upon inquiry
regarding the proposed e ffective date of the ordina nce, if approved, City Clerk Car ol Intlekofer
stated provisions of the ordinance indicate effectiveness would be upon approval of the ordinance
on 3  and final reading and publication of the ordinance in the local newspaper, which would berd
some time the week following final approval to allow time to finalize revised business and permit
application forms.  Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. Brown moved to approve on third and
final reading, seconded by Mr. Valdez.  Mr. Brown moved to amend by substitute, seconded by Ms.
Broyles.  I t was advised the  substitut e includes i nserting the words “ and public tra nsportation
conveyances” in Section 5.72.010, para graph I ., betwee n the words “ records” and “r elating.”
Discussion topi cs in cluded: L anguage c onsistency; va gueness o f w ording in c ertain s ections;
specification of fees relative to criminal or background checks; time frame of applicant disclosure
of convictions; language involving reasons for the chief’s disapproval of an application; definition
of terms contained in Section 5.72.060 A. 1. and whether the section applied to on-duty as well as
off-duty drivers; daily trip sheets and record keeping requirements; taxicab logos and approval of
same by the police chief; posting of driver permit with photo within conveyances; fees assessed for
transportation services; safety issues and concerns, and clarification of wording in certain sections
of the ordinance.  Ms. Broyles moved to postpone for 2 weeks, seconded by Mr. Valdez.  Dr. Rinne
raised a point of order regarding the procedural process involving the motion to amend by substitute
vs. the motion to postpone.  Upon inquiry regarding the amended substitute language, Dan White,
City Attorney, explained the change was made pursuant to discussion and recommendation by the
Finance Committee, noting previous changes were also included.  Following comments on voting
on the proposed substitute prior to the motion to postpone, Mayor Kaysen advised the motion to
amend by substitute would be the motion on the floor.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members
of the governing body with the exception of Ms. Ash, Mr. Brown and Dr. Rinne voting “no.”  Mayor
Kaysen advised the motion to amend by  substitute carried.  Pursuant to comments reg arding the
procedural process, Ms. Broyles withdrew her motion to postpone with concurrence by Mr. Valdez
as second to the motion.  Mr. Brown requested a point of clarification regarding what motion had
been voted upon with Mayor Kaysen stating the motion to amend by substitute was the motion acted
upon and approved.  Mr. Brown moved to postpone for 2 weeks (to August 9, 2010), seconded by
Ms. Broyles.  Mr. Elkins spoke in support of postponement and commented on ordinance sections
he felt should be clarified or reviewed for discrepancies.  Discussion included enforcement; whether
the postponement time frame would be adequate to review issues that had been raised; request to
Mr. Elkins to submit written comments for suggested changes, and what would transpire during the
postponement time frame regarding further review and potential changes to the ordinance.  Mayor
Kaysen confirmed the dates/times of Finance Committee and City Council meeting schedules on
the ordinance if the postponement was approved.  Upon inquiry, Lt. Mark Munari, Police Dept.
Patrol Division Commander, provided information on meetings and communications that had been
held with public transportation companies and the opportunity for their input regarding the proposed
ordinance, stating the ordinance was drafted pursuant to cab companies’ r equest and that their
comments had been taken into consideration when drafting the ordinance.  He advised he will work
with the City Attorney and public transportation companies to further review the ordinance during
the postponement time frame.  Comment was made on the proposed postponement time frame.  Mr.
Brown withdrew his motion to postpone for 2 weeks with concurrence by Ms. Broyles as second
to the motion.  Mr. Brown moved to postpone for 4 weeks, seconded by Mr. Valdez.  Mr. Elkins
spoke in support of the motion.  May or Kaysen clarified if the postponement  was a pproved, the
item would come before City Council at the August 23, 2010 meeting for referral back to Finance
Committee.  Motion to postpone (to August 23, 2010) carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the
governing body.
“An Ordinance annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, a parcel of land being  situated

a portion of Carla Drive, Thomas Road and College Drive, Laramie County, Wyoming (generally

 [CA] Consideration of Bid #M-24-10 for a contract between the City of Cheyenne and  Colorado

7:00 p.m. at Alf’s Pub, 1622 East 19th St., Cheyenne, WY, in conjunction with the Alf’s Pub Golf
Tournament. Proceeds to benefit the local Multiple Sclerosis Society.  
Announcement was made of  the  a ppointment o f D onald T hreewit to  the  Hi storic
Preservation Board to be confirmed August 9, 2010. 
Under other business, Steven Martinez, Bad Ass Tattoo, reviewed meetings, conversations,
governing body’s hearing process and outcome, and his subsequent appeal request to District Court,
which has be en dismissed, reg arding denial of his body  artist business per mit.  He expressed
procedural concerns involving the hearing, and commented on his approval through the County to
open a body art establishment outside of City limits.  He requested City Council approve issuance
(by means of taking a vote) of his body artist permit to p rovide services within the City.  Ci ty
Attorney Dan White requested the opportunity to research whether the governing body, under other
business at a City  Council meeting , has the a uthority to gr ant a lice nse based on a proc edural
posture.  Mr. Martinez commented on the Wyoming Administrative Procedure Act and the appeal
process.  Mr. White stated his belief that there was no prohibition in City Code that would preclude
Mr. Martinez from re-applying for a body artist permit.  Discussion followed on allowing time for
the City Attorney to review issues involved with  the request and a reasonable time frame for the
attorney’s response.  Mr. Mar tinez talked about his financial busine ss hardship, timeliness of a
response from the attorney, and potential litigation issues that may affect the City.  Co mments
followed on procedural process; timeliness of legal review; re-application for a permit as an option;
whether conditions could be placed on a license, and potential litigation pursuant to comments made
by Mr. Martinez.  Mayor Kaysen advised the City Attorney would review and provide a response
to Mr. Martinez and governing body members by the end of the week (i.e. July 30, 2010).
There being  no further  business to com e before the  g overning body , Mayor Ka ysen
adjourned the meeting at 7:10 p.m. 
 Submitted by,
 
 
 
 Carol Intlekofer, MMC
 City Clerk

who live within a one mile radius of City limits to express opposition to City Council’s one mile
jurisdictional authority (re: approval of plat s), indicating the g roup may check into whether the
authority is u nconstitutional.  H e c ited e xamples o f d evelopment c oncerns e ast o f t he City in
support of his statements.
Ms. Case expressed c oncerns with pas t annexation actions, some involving issues unde r
the 201 agreement, and stated her support of the City being a good neighbor to county residents.

suggested that the Committee of the Whole be reimplemented.
There being  no further  business to com e before the  g overning body , Mayor Ka ysen
adjourned the meeting at 7:55 p.m.

“An Ordinance amending Title 17, Zoning, Chapter 17.08, Zoning Districts Established
and Section 17.08.040 Chey enne city map of the Chey enne City Code, by  changing the zoning
classification from CB Community Business District to HI Heavy Industrial District for Lots 8 &
9, Bloc k 1, Chey enne B usiness Parkway , First Filing, Cheyenne, Wy oming ( located a t the
northeast corner  of the interse ction of Prog ress Circle  and Campstool Road).”  Repor ting f or
Public Services Committee, Mr. Collins moved to approve on third a nd final re ading, seconded
by Mr. Spiker.  Ms. Case called a point of order due to a quorum not being present at the Public
Services Committee meeting.  Upon inquiry from Mayor Kaysen, Ms. Case verified the purpose
of her point of order  was based on her position that a quorum of the Public Services Committee
was not present at the time the meeting was scheduled to begin, nor when the meeting was called
to order at a later time (City Clerk’s note:  Mee ting scheduled to begin at 12:00 p.m. on Oct . 5,
2010 did not convene until 1 2:16 p.m.), and that thre e members constitutes a quorum.  May or
Kaysen confirmed the Committee meeting  was called to order later than the sc heduled time and
that three Council members were present, and requested Dan White, City Attorney, provide his
opinion on the issue of whether a quorum was present at the meeting.  Mr. White advised he had
reviewed some standard policies of the City Council adopted in 2002 and that one policy relates
to when a quorum is not present at the time a standing  committee of the City Council meets.  He
stated the policy  pro vides that the Council’s executive assistant has the option of c ontacting
other City Council members and r equesting their attenda nce at the meeting in the a bsence of a
committee member  in order to ha ve a quor um present.  He  noted t his was the situation that
occurred a t the Public Services Committ ee m eeting a nd that Mr. Spiker wa s contac ted and
requested to attend the mee ting a t which time Mr. Collins and Dr. Rinne wer e pre sent.  Mr.
White stated his belief  that th e policy also provides that the Council member  who then attends
the standing  committee meeting  has the rig ht to vote on any  pendi ng matters bef ore the
committee.  He expressed his legal opinion that there was a quorum present at the time the
meeting commenced, however, there was not a quor um at 12:00 p.m., the time the meeting  was
scheduled, a nd a dvised, un der R obert’s Rul es o f O rder, tha t th e Ma yor ha s the  right to sta te
whether t he po int of  or der i s w ell t aken o r n ot w ell t aken, or  the  Ma yor c an a llow t he City
Council itself to rule on the point of order if the Mayor feels there is doubt regarding the matter.
He further advised if th e matter is submitted to a vote of the City  Council itself, the question
might be whether the Council rejects the recommendations of the Public Service s Committee on
agenda i tems a s r eported b y M r. C ollins.  In a ddition, s ince s tanding c ommittees a re o nly a
recommending bo dy, e ven if  City  Cou ncil c hooses to  re ject th e re commendations, M r. Whit e
expressed his belief that the City Council could proceed to consider the agenda matters.  During
further explanation on the point of order tha t she raised, Ms. Case referenced a previous incident
in 2008 occur ring at a standing  committee meeting involving a Counc il member, who wa s not
assigned as a member of the committee, who requested to be par t of the committee a nd was not
allowed to  pa rticipate in t he c ommittee members’ d iscussion o r v ote.  Sh e no ted th e City
requested and pa id for leg al research and a n opinion on the issue from a loca l attorney at the
time, and provided Mr . White with a c opy of the opinion .  Following review of the opinion and
upon rec eiving additional information from Ms. Case on c ircumstances involv ed in the 2008
incident, Mr. White stated  the pre vious 2008 situation appe ared to be slightly  different as the
opinion addre sses a for mer membe r of Council, who wa s not an assig ned member  of the
committee, appearing at the committee mee ting a nd wishing to be  involved in Comm ittee
members’ debate and voting, and that the current issue being discussed pertained to a Council
member being contacted and requested to attend a Committee meeting for purposes of a quorum
who then, under  the Cou ncil’s policy, is allowed to deba te and vote.  F urther information was
provided by Ms. Case and inc luded recitation of the outside leg al counsel’s interpretation of the
phrase “ele cted of ficial” contained in the opinion and involving City  Code Section 2.04.145.
Following c omments b y o ther m embers o f C ity C ouncil r elative t o s tanding c ommittees’
composition invol ving a  quo rum, committee member  assig nments and purpose  of the 2008
outside legal opinion, Mr. Whi te reiterated his positi on that the 2008 leg al opinion dealt with a
separate issue a nd his opinion that a quorum was pr esent a t the Public Services Committ ee
meeting in question.  L inda Allard, a gent for E choStar, req uested the g overning body  move
forward with the vote on the zone change ordinance on third reading, whether with or without, if
allowed, a  rec ognized recommenda tion from the Public Services Committee meeting .  Upon
inquiry as to whether postponement of the item for two weeks would affect EchoStar’s project,
she advised a  two wee k post ponement, at this tim e, would not af fect their curr ent plans for
development.  Comments followed on those Public Service s Committee members present at the
scheduled time of the meeting, Robert’s Rules of Order and recommendations from a committee,
and re ason for the committee meeting  starting  at a time later  than sche duled.  May or Ka ysen
recognized and revie wed the inf ormation that had be en presented and discussed re lative to t he
point of order that had been raised, and ruled the point of order was not well taken and would not
be a ccepted.  Com ments we re made in appreciation o f E choStar’s p resence in C heyenne, it s
commitment to the community  and support for  its expansion plans, and zoning uses allowed for
the property pursuant to information conta ined in the staff  report referencing past zoning action
for a  pr oposed u se a s a n e arly c hildhood c enter t hat ha s n ot m oved f orward in de velopment.
James Perot McNeil spoke in favor of discussing the item at a nother Public Services Committee
meeting to eliminate any  procedural confusion with Mayor Kaysen clarifying the ordinance ha d
been pr eviously revie wed a t tw o committee and two City  Council meeting s.  Ms. Allard
requested Council move forwa rd with approva l of the ordinanc e.  Comment followed re garding
the prior zone change from HI to CB and the r eason for changing it back to an HI zone.  Motion
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present. (#3908)
“An Ordinance creating Chapter 17.116.151 of Title 17, Zoning, of the Cheyenne City
Code pertaining to wat er hauler truck tempora ry loading sites.” Matt Ashby , Planning Ser vices
Director, reviewed the purpose of the or dinance in conjunction with rec ent oil exploration work
and staff ’s initial belief that timeliness for the or dinance t o enac t emerg ency-type re gulations
was ne eded, and stated re cently curr ent location options had bee n made a vailable f or wa ter
haulers to acc ess wate r which ha s dimini shed the immediac y to act upon t he ordinance.  He
requested the item be withdra wn from the agenda to allow for a he aring process and review and

October 25, 2010
The Governing Body of the City  of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date be ginning

MEMBERS - Ms. Ash, Mr. B rown, Ms. B royles, Ms. Case, Mr. Collins, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Pierson,
and Mr. Spiker. Absent:  Mr . Valdez.  A lso present: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk; Barb Dorr, City
Treasurer, and Dan White, City Attorney.  The pledge of allegiance was recited.
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items l isted with the desig nation of [CA]  are considered to be

discussion on these items unless a member  of the g overning body so reque sts and support by  two
other members is r eceived.  An y item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its
normal sequence on the agenda.) Mr. Collins m oved to appr ove Consent Ag enda items, sec onded
by Mr. Spiker.  Motion carried. Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.

Mr. Brown moved to approve pa yment of vouche rs as pre sented, seconded by Mr. Spiker.
Upon inquiry, Mayor Kaysen, City Treasurer Barb Dorr and Planning Services Director Matt Ashby

applications, case, and protection plan in the amount of $691.01 included in a voucher payable to

(MPO) with federal funding and that the equipment, which provides more flexibility and portability
than a  la ptop c omputer, and software wi ll b e uti lized f or the  MPO  on -site Em ployment D ata
Collection Project.  Dr. Rinne move d to divide the question to allow for sepa rate action on the
purchase of the iTunes component in the amount of $54.01, seconded by Ms. Ash.  Motion carrie d.
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.  Dr. Rinne moved to postpone action on
the pay ment of $54.01 for iTunes for one  wee k (to Nov. 1, 2010), se conded by  Ms. Ash.
Comments followed regarding the purpose of the iPad purchase and what applications software was
acquired through iTunes.  Ms. Dorr advised of payment time frame options relative to the vendor’s
invoice due date, and Mr. Ashby clarified that he had confirmed with the MPO Director that the
programs downloaded via iTunes involved word processing, spreadsheets a nd p resentation
programs.  Motion to postpone failed.  Voting  “yes” – Ms. Ash, Dr . Rinne and Mr. Spiker.  Voting
“no” – Mr . Br own, M s. Br oyles, Ms. Case, Mr. Collins, Mr. Pi erson, a nd May or Ka ysen.  Mr.
Collins moved to app rove payment of the Apple iTunes portion of the pur chase order, seconded by
Mr. Brown. Discussion followed on the purpose a nd necessity of purchasing an iPad and affiliated
software items.  Motion to approve carried.  Voting “yes” – Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Ms. Case, Mr.
Collins, Mr. Pierson, and May or Ka ysen.  Voting  “no” – Ms. Ash, D r. Rinne and Mr. Spiker .
Discussion on remaining vouchers followed.  Upon inquiry regarding a voucher payable to Service

damage resulting from the fire at the Hitching Post Inn and whether the cost might be recoverable
through insurance, City Attorney Dan White advised a claim could be asserted and stated the matter

Upon inquiry  whether  any  other da mage wa s incurred  due to the fire, Rick Par ish, Parks and

from fire extinguishing efforts and indicated he had compiled a list and submitt ed it to the May or

“An Ordinance annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, a parcel of land being situated
in t he SW1/4 of Section 2 5, T. 14N., R. 66W., 6th  P.M ., L aramie Cou nty, Wy oming (g enerally

“A Resolu tion re-appropriating the  fiscal y ear 2011 budg et of the City  of Chey enne
according to the provisions of W.S. 16-4-112 & 16-4-113.” Upon query to members of the
governing body by Mayor Kaysen regarding a brief statement he wished to make c oncerning the
re-appropriation, and there  being no o bjection raised, Mayor Kaysen provided inf ormation on a
proposed employ ee r ecognition pay ment included in the re -appropriation.  He e xplained the
intent was to recognize full-time employees for their efforts this past calendar year and stated the
payment was not intended to  include the May or or membe rs of the g overning body . May or
Kaysen noted other items included in the proposed r e-appropriation and concluded his statement
by indicating he woul d have no objection if the e mployee recognition payment was not par t of
the re-appropriation.  Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. Brown moved to adopt, seconded by
Mr. Pierson.  Mr. B rown moved to a mend by substitute, seconded by Mr. Collins.  Mr. Br own
clarified the substitute removes the line item for the employee recognition payment, which will
change the total re-appropriation amount.  Planning Ser vices Director Matt Ashby, Public Works
Director Jim El ias, City  Tr easurer B arb Do rr a nd Ma yor Ka ysen p rovided information in
response to inquiry and discussion that included the following topics: Request for an additional
planner position in the Building/Development Office to assist with i ncreased workload involving
building pe rmits a nd sit e pla ns; st aff re ductions, r eorganization a nd re assignment o f d uties in
several City  de partments du e to t he re duction in  wo rkforce a nd vo luntary e arly re tirements
occurring earlier in the y ear; effect of re duced staffing levels on customer  service, maintenance
of fleet vehicles and equipment (safety) and high volume areas (sanitation, street and alley); part-
time employment services being received by the Public Wor ks Department by a for mer director
of the department; staff reassignments made to assist with coverage; outsourcing of certain Flee t
Maintenance re pair ser vices a nd chang es to the di vision’s preve ntative vehicle s/equipment
maintenance sche dules; re- appropriation line item involvi ng juvenile justice g rants; staff ing
levels and review of same prior to a nd during the Fiscal Year 2011-2012 budget process; time
frame involved in the def erred $1,000,000 pay ment to the Boa rd of Public Utilit ies, and
administrative process utilized when determining whether to fill a vacated staff position.  Motion
to amend ca rried.  V oting “y es” –  a ll members of the g overning body  prese nt. Additional
comment of concern was made regarding staffing levels.  Main motion as amended by substitute
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.  (#5247)
“A Resolution authorizing the Ma yor and the  Ci ty Clerk to sign a F inal Plat for Teton
Lumber, a replat of vacated portions of Lots 3, 4 & 5, Block 29; and Lots 1 & 2, Block 30; and
McComb Avenue, O riginal City of Chey enne; a nd a portion of L ot 3, Section 36, T.14N.,
R.67W., 6th P.M., C heyenne, Wyoming (located at the northwe st corner of the interse ction of
Cribbon Avenue a nd W. 27th S treet).”  Reporting f or Pub lic Se rvices Co mmittee, M s. As h
moved to adopt with staff r ecommendations #1 throug h 4, seconde d by  Mr. Collins. M otion
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.  (#5248)
“A Resolution authorizing the M ayor and the City  Clerk to sign a  Final Plat for T he
Bluffs, 8th Filing, situated in a portion of the SW¼ N W¼ of Section 21, T.14N., R.66W ., 6th
P.M., Cheyenne, Wyoming (located east of and a djacent to Ogden Road, between Apache Street
and Pla in V iew R oad).”  Ke lly Ha fner, B enchmark En gineers, a dvised h e wa s in volved w ith
investigating drainage issues for  the dev eloper, United Western Corpora tion, affiliated with the
project, and submitted a map diag ram (on f ile with City Clerk’s off ice) depicting  a propose d
regional detention fa cility for the a rea.  Mr. Haf ner referenced recommendations relative to the
preliminary plat; reviewed areas contained in the map diagram, and proposed consideration of a
regional drainage detention facility vs. small pocket detention area(s).  He  discussed constraints
of the area based on his research of drainage flow direction, topography and run-off volumes, and
the dif ficulty of  e xtending Pla inview R oad to  Con verse Av e. d ue to d rainage flows and City
water main issues; ex plained logistics involving a r egional facility vs. multiple smaller detention
ponds, and noted a ffiliated maintenance responsibility issues.  Discussion included the following
topics:  Potential interim detention lot, depicted a s L ot 10, Block 11 on the  plat map, and
developer c onstruction o ptions; r un-off v olumes d raining i nto t he C ity s torm s ewer s ystem;
issues a nd c oncerns in volving the pr oposed re gional de tention fa cility, w hich w ould c urrently
include prope rty in the county  (not within Cit y limit s) over  small pocket ar eas; phy sical
characteristics of L ot 10, Block 11; improveme nts to drainage sy stem, and extension of
Plainview Road to Converse Ave.  Frank Cole, United Western Corporation, developer, provided
information on water drainage in the area; cited other development projects involving drainage
issues, and stated a s development to the north a dvances the intent would be for  th e area to be
annexed.  Doug  Vetter, City Engineer, stated  the recommendation of de velopment staff is that
Lot 10, Block 11 be reserved for drainage on the final plat; not necessarily dedicated as drainage.
He noted pote ntial concerns with the location and d esign of the regional facility, and pre clusion
of the extension of Plainview Road which could affect the area’s transportation network.  Ms.
Ash moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. Collins.  M s. Ash moved to postpone for two weeks (to
Nov. 8, 2010), sec onded by  Mr. Collins. I t was advised the  postponem ent would allow for
continuing discussion on future road connectivity and drainage.  Co mments followed regarding
drainage and Plainview Roa d extension concerns; re serve vs. dedica te L ot 10, Block 11 f or
drainage; detention area(s) maintenance responsibility, and small local detention areas generally.
Upon inquiry, Ci ty Attorney Dan Wh ite explained t he recommendation noted by Mr. Vetter is
being reque sted to be plac ed on the plat to ensur e dra inage issues for  this specific plat a re
addressed by reserving the lot for detention purposes.  He added that the developer may desire to
retain the lot in private ownership and that the City’s intent is to reserve the lot, not dedicate it to
the City.  Mr. Cole stated his advoc acy for a   regional detention pond to be maintaine d by the
City, and noted liability  risks to property  owners involving owne rship of small detention pond
areas.  He spoke in opposition to t he staff recommendation to “tag” Lot 10, Block 11 as reserved
and stated his intention is not to even use L ot 10 for drainage. Comments followed in support of
regional detention ponds, conne ctivity of ea st-west roads for future use, loc al elementary school
traffic, and problems with incremental developments.  Motion to postpone failed.  Voting  “yes” –
Dr. Rinne.  Voting “no” – Ms. Ash, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Ms. Case, Mr. Collins, Mr. Pierson,
Mr. Spiker and Mayor Kaysen.  Mr. Collins moved to amend to include staff  recommendation
#1, seconde d by Mr. B rown, whic h rea ds: “I ndicate Lot 10, Bloc k 11 as an a rea rese rved f or
storm water detention on final plat map”.  Discussion followed on a regional detention facility vs.
interim local detention ponds; potential route alternatives to provide connector street to Converse
Ave.; physical natural depth of Lot 10, Block 11 relative to drainage use; proximity of dete ntion
facility/pond(s) to an elementary school in the area and concerns with traffic and safety of
students; use of L ot 10, Block 11 f or drainage during plat development; nee d for f uture
discussion regarding the regional detention facility, and unattractiveness of local detention areas.
During discussion on alternate routes to extend street traffic to Converse Ave., Mr. Cole provided
information concerning the Water Tank Bluff street area.  U pon inquiry, Mr. Hafner, provided
information on the depth of Lot 10, Block 11, and size of the proposed regional detention facility,
stating it w ould be a “bare minimal size” wi th a gravity drain system.  M otion to a mend with
staff r ecommendation #1 carr ied.  Voting  “yes” – a ll members of the  governing body prese nt
with the exception of Ms. Ash, Ms. Broy les, Dr. Ri nne and May or Kaysen voting “no”.  U pon
inquiry conce rning whether  a bar rier or  fenc ing of  side slopes of L ot 10, Block 11 would be
installed, Mr. Hafner stated the side slopes do not re quire a barrier, and at this tim e no barrier or
fencing is planned and the area would be open.  Main motion as amended carried.  Voting “yes”
– all members of the governing body present with the exception of Dr. Rinne and Mayor Kaysen
voting “no”. (#5249)
[CA] Agreement between the City of Cheyenne, Office of Youth Alternatives, and Laramie    
County, for juvenile probation services. Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried.  (#5603)
[CA] Contract between the City of Chey enne, Office of Youth Alterna tives, and the State of
Wyoming, Department of Family Services, for intensive support mentoring program for youth in

Contract between the City of Cheyenne and WINhealth Partners for city employees health
insurance coverage. Mr. B rown moved to a pprove, seconded by Mr. Spiker. Upon inquiry , Rich
Wiederspahn, Huma n Resource s Director, provide d information on the staff  I nsurance
Committee review and evaluation process, and explained high medical claims experience during
the past year as well as the new federal health care act have contributed to the 12.6% coverage
cost increase.  He discussed keeping health care premiums and deductible amounts affordable for
employees; diff erent cover age options available  under the  plan, and the eff ect of the fe deral
Affordable Care Act (ACA) on increased provider costs.  Ste ve Goldstone, WINhealth Partners,
advised WINhealth worked with actuaries and industry experts to project estimated increases due
to the impact of the A CA, noting a ny time benef its expand there is some cost involved.  Upon
query, M r. Wie derspahn st ated h e wa s u naware of  wh ether C ity e mployees w ere e ligible to
participate in the State' s health insuranc e prog ram.  Discussion fo llowed and included the
following topics:  Amount of high medic al claims incurr ed within t he past y ear; degree of staff
due dil igence c onducted in  re viewing a nd e valuating a  he alth pla n; lack of seeking pr oposals
from other providers; continuing costs of health care coverage and budgetary con cerns;
percentage of  pr emium c ost pa id b y the  City  vs . e mployees; d eductible/co-pay a mounts;
differences between shelter, silver and gold plans; stress incurred by employees within the past
year due to budgetary reductions; employee wellness (coaching) program and affiliated costs, and
continuing ne ed to re view how to curtail future  cost incre ases.  Thr oughout discussion, Mr.
Wiedersphahn and Mr. Goldstone provided inf ormation available in response to questions.  Barb
Dorr, City Treasurer, advised the e stimated cost of implementing  a we llness/coaching program
for employees last year was $50,000, at which time the City was offered to be included in a pilot
program through WINhealth.  She estimated the curr ent cost to implement  a program would be
double that amount.  Up on further inquiry, Mr. Goldstone clarified that the catastrophic claims
the City  had last y ear would not have be en impacte d by  having  a we llness prog ram. Mr.
Wiederspahn ve rified the Committee did not resea rch pla ns offe red by  other he alth insurance
companies dur ing the  plan re view/renewal proc ess, and state d cata strophic illnesses and
accidents that occurred last year  has driven up premiums which could also affect coverage costs
by other provide rs.  He disc ussed upcoming employee health education sessions; nurse hotline
assistance program currently in p lace, and other health awareness and training for employees to
hopefully assist in reducing  coverage costs.  Upon inquiry  concerning the percentage increase of
catastrophic claims incurr ed in the past y ear, Mr. Wiederspahn ind icated he did not have  that
information available at the mee ting and would provide it.  Comments followed on continuing
research reg arding a we llness prog ram.  Motion car ried. Voting  "y es" – all members of  the
governing body with the exception of Ms. Case voting "no".  (#5614)

Equipment for the City  of C heyenne Fire & Re scue D epartment -- re ferred to F inance
Committee. 
Consideration of a pr oposal for a n agreement, recommended by the Art  In Public Pl aces
Committee,  to ac quire artwork to be k nown as “Hare Raising” from Tim Cherry, Branson, MO,
for placement at the e ntrance area of the David R. Romero South Chey enne Community Park.
John Goodier, Chair man, Art I n Public Pl aces Committee (AIPPC), reviewed remaining budget
funding ( approximately $ 20,000.00) f or a rtwork a cquisition, th e Co mmittee’s p urpose and
directive to seek artwork for the community pursuant to City Code, AIPPC’s efforts in reviewing
and recommen ding an appropriate art piece for the new park, and the artist’s offer to provide the
sculpture at a reduced c ost ($20,000.00).  Upon inquiry , Mr. Goodier  discussed the AI PPC’s
understanding of funding availability due to budg etary concerns; the number of  sculptures that
have be en placed in the community under the program; status of the Committee (active vs .
inactive) if funding  is not available; sculpture proposals and se lection proc ess utiliz ed by  the
Committee; ar tists’ will ingness to offer  their s culptures at a r educed cost in order  to have the
artwork publicly  display ed; private  donations that have be en provide d for some pie ces, a nd
Committee member s’ and City  staff a ssistance, at times, in phy sical installation of artwor k.
Upon further  inquiry, he reviewed personal costs that Mr. Cherry incurred during the proposal
process regarding the sculpture for the park. Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. Brown stated
there was no re commendation on the item fr om the Com mittee.  Mr. Collins moved to approve ,
seconded by Ms. Case. Ms. Br oyles distributed a broc hure (on file with offic e of City Clerk) on
sculptures that have been placed throughout the City.  D iscussion included the following topics:
Support for volunteer members serving on the City’s various boards and committees; timing  of
the e xpenditure due to b udgetary c oncerns, e conomic sit uation, re ductions in s taffing le vels
incurred earlier in the year, and increasing costs to employees without compensation adjustment;
past and current status of budg et funding for artwork; current fiscal year budget review affiliated
with the funding  line item; budge t priorities and fisca l responsibility; community beautification;
support for sculpture at the new park; funding of basic infrastructure needs (programs, services,
staffing and oper ating costs, etc.)  vs. purcha sing artwork at this t ime; employees’ perception of
purchasing a rtwork r elative to budg et re ductions and conc erns, a nd possible ut ilization of the
funding for the artwork for other City budgets if the proposal is not approved. Motion to approve
failed (due to a tie vote).   Voting “yes” – Ms. Broy les, Ms. Case, Mr. Collins, Mr. Valdez and
Mayor Kaysen.  Voting “no” – Ms. Ash, Mr. Brown, Mr. Pierson, Dr. Rinne and Mr. Spiker.  
[CA] Consideration o f purch ase of a use d Jac obson mo wer f rom C &  M G olf &  Gr ounds,
Denver, CO, for the Grounds and Facilities Division (2007-2010 1% Sales a nd Use Tax. Motion
(per Consent Agenda) carried (amount not to exceed $42,350.00).
Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held December 13, 2010 at 6:00 p.m.
in City Council Chambers for a  transfer of owne rship and tra nsfer of loca tion for a r etail liquor
license a pplication filed with the City  Clerk' s Offic e for  W. E. Dinneen, I nc. d/b/a [to be
determined], 400 W. Lincolnway, Cheyenne, WY. (License currently held by Cheyenne 28, LLC
f/d/b/a C.B. & Potts, 1650 Dell Range Boulevard, Cheyenne, Wyoming.) 
Under other business, discussion was he ld on a proposed “ write-off” listing that had been
submitted to members of the governing body by the City Treasurer for uncollectible debts due the
City totaling $85,579.00 for years 2003-2008.  Topics discussed included: Collection procedures;
recognition that many  on the debt listing are active businesses in the community ; suggestion of
initiating small claims court a ctions to seek pay ments due throug h assistance of City legal staff
and staff  time that may  be involved; whethe r City service s could be res tricted to those owing
money and setting  a limit of indebtedness fo r same; certa in billi ngs issued throug h Boa rd of
Public Ut ilities, a nd the  do llar amount i nvolving no n-sufficient f unds c hecks that constitutes a
felony o ffense.  D uring d iscussion, Barb D orr, Ci ty T reasurer, e xplained p ast a nd c urrent de bt
collection procedures and communication and collection efforts by staff; that some a mounts due
are unpaid accrued interest; collection agency services, and different types of debt areas, and
noted the listing wa s provided for  review prior to a r esolution being submitted to write- off the
debts.
There being no further business to c ome before the governing body, Mayor Kay sen
adjourned the meeting at 7:04 p.m.
Submitted by,
Carol Intlekofer, MMC
City Clerk

Hailey El lingham, s poke in o pposition to t he pe nding re moval of  de signated trees in
Lions Park due to height concerns affiliated with the air flight path for the airport.  She discussed
positive attributes of the par k involving preservation of habitat areas for birds and other wildlife;
trees serving as a sound ba rrier and a w ind break around the  lake; we tlands environment; ef fect
of the tre es on the environmental integrity of the pa rk, and c ommunity quality of life involving
park a esthetics.  She requested the opportunity to review documentation relative to whether the
trees posed an a ir flig ht safety  conce rn, and r equested the dec ision to remove the tre es be
reconsidered.  Jackie Oceanak also spoke in opposition to removal of the tre es, remarking on tree
benefits to the community, and requested the removal of the trees be delayed to allow for review
of further information involving slope mea surements relative to Federal Aviation Administration
(FAA) regulations and City ordinance.  M s. Oceanak read a letter into the record (on file with
City Clerk’s office) that she had submitted on behalf of her citizen’s group  t o the City Council
on Dec. 1 0, 20 10 regarding the  group’s c oncerns and issues su rrounding removal of  the  tr ees.
Other topics pr esented by Ms. Oceanak included: Unr esolved issues about the tr ees; actual tree
heights; wording c ontained in City Ordinance No. 1969; heig ht restrictions and slope
measurements relative to t he flight path; whether grand stand structures at Frontier Park violate
height restriction, and the  City vs. FAA ’s authority and responsibility regarding removal of the
trees.  Ms. Ocea nak submitted copies ( on file with City  Clerk’s off ice) of a sur vey map of the
Lions Park are a in question, noting the  map was pr ovided b y the Airport B oard, and reviewed
information containe d on the map. May or Ka ysen re quested Ms. Oceanak submit the gr oup’s
questions in writing to the May or’s Of fice along  with copies of r eferenced materials noted by
Ms. Ocea nak during  her c omments.  I t was questioned whe ther a  perf ormance bond had be en
required a s p art o f t he c ontract w ith t he c ontractor h ired t o r emove t he t rees.  U pon i nquiry,
Mayor Kaysen advised the tree company hired to provide removal services is licensed to do work
in the City, that no performance bond was required and that the contract work is identified to start
in January. Dan White, City  Attorney, stated all City  licensed tree service providers are required
to car ry commerc ial general liability  insurance  as a c ondition of a business license, a nd the
amount of liabil ity coverage required under the c ontract had bee n increased from that requir ed
for a license.  Comments of conc ern were expressed regarding Council members listening to the
public, and compliance with Ordinance #1969.
There being no further business to c ome before the governing body, Mayor K aysen
adjourned the meeting at 7:30 p.m.
 Submitted by,
 Carol Intlekofer, MMC
 City Clerk
